## Cards

### Eval / Test / Exam
- `eval` | word | width 11 (wide) | native music+screen ^eval
- AI: a structured test of what a model can do or will do, including dangerous-capability tests before release. Origin: dangerous-capability evals: ARC Evals (now METR) on GPT-4, 2023; UK AI Security Institute (founded Nov 2023 as AI Safety Institute); 'evaluation awareness', models noticing they are being tested, reported in 2025 system cards [high]
- senses: **school** an exam; a graded test ("pencils down, the exam is over"); **body** a medical test or checkup ("the test came back negative"); **music** the sound engineer's mic check ("testing, one, two"); **screen** a screen test: an actor's audition on camera ("she nailed the screen test"); **faith-myth** a trial of faith (Abraham, Job) ("God was testing him"); **love** testing a partner's commitment ("was that a test?"); **money** a bank stress test ("the banks passed the stress test"); **sport-games** Test cricket: the five-day international match ("the second Test"); **war** a weapons test ("a nuclear test"); **law-state** a qualifying exam (bar exam, driving test) ("I failed my driving test")
- idioms: testing, one two, put to the test, test the waters, pass with flying colors, this is only a test, the acid test, stand the test of time
- anchors: 'This is a test of the Emergency Broadcast System': US TV/radio test announcement (1963-1997); Evaluation awareness: 2025 system cards report models saying 'I think I'm being tested'; Kobayashi Maru: Star Trek's unwinnable test that Kirk passed by reprogramming it
- pivots (jep drafts): "Testing, one, two; it said, I know this is a test" (ai+music+school) / "God tested Job; Job never asked if it was an eval" (ai+faith-myth) / "Was that a test? Everything's a test now" (ai+love+school) / "Reprogrammed the Kobayashi Maru, like Kirk, like it" (ai+screen+school)
- enact: music: the track opens with a real mic check ('testing, one two') that turns out to be the vocal hook | picture: an exam hall where one desk holds a laptop that glances up at the invigilator | type: lines appear in a multiple-choice layout, the 'right' bubble filling itself
- sound: 2 syl, e-VAL (test: TEST); rhymes (test) best, rest, chest, west, nest, guest, confessed, obsessed; slant (eval) pal, gal, canal, morale
- stakes mind, jobs, species | registers funny, ominous, panicked
- neighbors: [[#^benchmark|Benchmark]] (same-family), [[#^turing-test|Turing Test / Imitation Game]] (same-family), [[#^sandbagging|Sandbagging]] (pairs-with), [[#^red-team|Red Team]] (pairs-with), [[#^curriculum|Curriculum]] (pairs-with)
- refs: 3 of 22 songs: Nothing Went Foom! 2, LGTM (Looks Good To Me) 1, The Simulation's Ending 1 | KC 0
- risk: 'eval' is jargon; 'test' carries the senses; 'this is only a test' is common
- note: Ten domains, and the mic check means the song itself can start as a test and become the hook.

### Alignment
- `alignment` | word | width 11 (wide) | native music | Idi seed ^alignment
- AI: making a model want and do what its makers and users actually intend. Origin: Wiener 1960, 'Some Moral and Technical Consequences of Automation' (the problem stated); Russell ~2014 'value alignment'; 'AI alignment' spread via MIRI and Christiano around 2016 [medium]
- senses: **everyday** arranged in a straight line ("the chairs are out of alignment"); **craft** a car's wheels set to the same angle ("my car needs an alignment"); **craft** text set flush left, right or centered ("center-align the title"); **body** joints and spine stacked in good posture ("keep your spine in alignment"); **work** agreement on goals inside a team ("let's get aligned before the meeting"); **law-state** the political bloc a nation sides with ("the Non-Aligned Movement"); **sport-games** a character's moral axis in role-playing games ("lawful good alignment"); **faith-myth** fate lining up in your favour, from astrology ("the stars aligned"); **science** planets appearing in a line from Earth ("planetary alignment"); **music** lining recorded tracks up in time and phase ("phase-align the drum mics"); **love** partners sharing values and plans ("we're aligned on kids")
- idioms: the stars aligned, out of alignment, get aligned, fall in line, lawful good, chaotic evil, on the same page
- anchors: Norbert Wiener, 1960: 'we had better be quite sure that the purpose put into the machine is the purpose which we really desire'; D&D alignment chart: the nine-square lawful/chaotic, good/evil grid from Advanced Dungeons & Dragons (1977), now a meme template; Non-Aligned Movement: Cold War bloc of states siding with neither superpower, founded Belgrade 1961
- pivots (jep drafts): "Spent a fortune on my alignment, still pulling to the left" (ai+craft+law-state) / "Lawful good on the chart, chaotic in the weights" (ai+sport-games) / "The stars aligned for us. The model never did." (ai+love+faith-myth) / "Straighten up, they said. Alignment is a posture now." (ai+body)
- enact: music: two out-of-phase takes of the same vocal drift apart, then snap into phase on the hook word | picture: a car on a wheel-alignment rig, laser lines on the tyres, the readout screen showing a model's value chart | type: a ragged line of words slides into one clean justified block on the beat
- sound: 3 syl, a-LIGN-ment; rhymes confinement, refinement, assignment, consignment; slant requirement, retirement, excitement, enlightenment, environment
- stakes species, mind, state | registers ominous, funny, hopeful
- neighbors: [[#^values|Values]] (pairs-with), [[#^mesa-optimizer|Mesa-Optimizer / Inner Alignment]] (part-of), [[#^corrigibility|Corrigibility]] (same-family), [[#^constitution|Constitution]] (part-of), [[#^reward-hacking|Reward Hacking]] (opposite)
- refs: 1 of 22 songs: Count Me In 2 | KC 0
- risk: Corporate 'alignment' and AI 'alignment' blur; the D&D chart is heavily memed, so twist it
- note: Every sense means lining up with a reference you did not choose: the road, the spine, the team, the stars, the makers.

### Scale / Scaling (laws)
- `scaling` | word | width 11 (wide) | native music ^scaling
- AI: the finding that loss falls predictably as model size, data and compute grow together, so bigger runs get reliably better. Origin: Kaplan et al. 2020, 'Scaling Laws for Neural Language Models' (OpenAI); Hoffmann et al. 2022, 'Training Compute-Optimal Large Language Models' (Chinchilla, about 20 tokens per parameter) https://arxiv.org/abs/2001.08361 [high]
- senses: **music** an ordered ladder of notes; practicing it ("practice your scales"); **everyday** a device for weighing ("step on the scale"); **law-state** the balance held by Justice ("tip the scales"); **nature** the plates on a fish or a snake ("scales like armor"); **sport-games** to climb a wall or a mountain ("scale the cliff"); **craft** the ratio between a model or map and the real thing ("a 1:100 scale model"); **money** growth where cost per unit falls as size grows ("economies of scale"); **body** tartar scraped off teeth; dead skin flaking ("scale and polish at the dentist"); **mind** a rating from low to high ("on a scale of one to ten"); **faith-myth** scales falling from the eyes: sudden understanding (Acts 9:18) ("the scales fell from my eyes")
- idioms: tip the scales, on a scale of one to ten, economies of scale, the scales fell from my eyes, practice your scales, at scale, scale back, doesn't scale
- anchors: Scaling Laws for Neural Language Models: Kaplan et al. 2020: smooth power laws linking loss to size, data and compute https://arxiv.org/abs/2001.08361; Chinchilla: DeepMind 2022: most big models were undertrained; scale data with parameters https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.15556; Scales of Justice: Themis/Justitia's balance, emblem of courts
- pivots (jep drafts): "I practiced scales until the scales fell off my eyes" (ai+music+faith-myth) / "On a scale of one to ten, we scaled to eleven" (ai+mind+screen) / "Put the planet on the scale and kept on scaling" (ai+everyday+nature) / "Your love doesn't scale; mine does, at a loss" (ai+love+money)
- enact: music: a major scale climbs one octave per chorus, each run on a bigger instrument (toy piano, piano, organ, full orchestra) | picture: a bathroom scale whose needle is a loss curve, a fish-scale texture on the GPU heat sink | type: each repeat of the word set one size larger, stair-stepping off the frame
- sound: 1 syl, SCALE; rhymes fail, sale, whale, tale, rail, jail, pale, hail; slant fall, skill, shell, scaled
- stakes wallet, planet, species | registers awe, ominous, smug
- neighbors: [[#^compute|Compute]] (causes), [[#^bitter-lesson|The Bitter Lesson]] (pairs-with), [[#^kardashev|Kardashev Scale]] (same-family), [[#^exponentials|Exponential(s)]] (pairs-with), [[#^loss-function|Loss / Loss Function]] (pairs-with), [[#^key|Key]] (pairs-with)
- refs: 0 of 22 songs | KC 0
- risk: 'scale is all you need' is worn on AI Twitter; lean on the music and justice senses instead
- note: Ten domains in one syllable, and two of them (musical scale, scale of justice) can be performed on screen and in the mix at once.

### Apple / Tree of Knowledge
- `apple` | word | width 10 (wide) | native music+screen ^apple
- AI: the forbidden fruit of knowledge, used for AI's temptation; also Apple Inc., its 'Apple Intelligence', and its 2025 paper doubting model reasoning. Origin: Genesis 2-3 (the fruit is unnamed; 'apple' comes via Latin malum, evil/apple); Apple Intelligence, WWDC June 2024; Shojaee et al. (Apple) June 2025, 'The Illusion of Thinking' [high]
- senses: **faith-myth** the fruit of the tree of knowledge of good and evil ("forbidden fruit"); **school** an apple for the teacher ("an apple for the teacher"); **science** Newton's falling apple and gravity ("Newton's apple"); **family** a cherished child ("the apple of my eye"); **screen** Snow White's poisoned apple (1937) ("one bite"); **body** Adam's apple; an apple a day ("an apple a day keeps the doctor away"); **music** Apple Records (the Beatles); Apple Music ("on Apple Music"); **money** Apple Inc., among the most valuable companies ("Apple stock"); **everyday** New York, the Big Apple; a bad apple ("one bad apple")
- idioms: apple of my eye, forbidden fruit, an apple a day, one bad apple, the apple doesn't fall far from the tree, how do you like them apples, apples to apples, upset the apple cart
- anchors: Turing's apple: Turing died in 1954 beside a half-eaten apple; the claim that Apple's logo honors him is a myth denied by its designer; The Illusion of Thinking: Apple researchers, June 2025, on reasoning models collapsing on harder puzzles
- pivots (jep drafts): "One bite of the apple and it knew good from evil" (ai+faith-myth+money) / "An apple for the teacher; the teacher was a phone" (ai+school+money) / "The apple doesn't fall far from the training set" (ai+family+science) / "Apple of my eye, Apple in my pocket" (ai+family+money)
- enact: music: a crisp apple-bite sample as the snare on the backbeat | picture: a phone-logo apple on a tree branch, a hand reaching; the bite mark is the cursor | type: the word with one letter bitten out of it
- sound: 2 syl, AP-ple; rhymes chapel, grapple, dapple, scrapple; slant battle, cattle, rattle, label, candle
- stakes mind, wallet, heart | registers tender, funny, ominous
- neighbors: [[#^creator|Creator / Creation]] (same-family), [[#^prometheus|Prometheus / Fire]] (same-family), [[#^teacher-student|Teacher / Student (models)]] (pairs-with), [[#^reasoning|Reasoning / Thinking]] (pairs-with), [[#^golem|Golem]] (same-family)
- refs: 1 of 22 songs: What Counts 1 | KC 0
- risk: the Bible never says apple; the Turing-logo story is a myth; Apple is a trademark, keep brand use descriptive
- note: Nine domains with fixed idioms in most; 'the apple doesn't fall far from the tree' is a ready line about training data.

### Bubble
- `bubble` | word | width 10 (wide) | native music+screen | Idi seed ^bubble
- AI: the fear that AI valuations and datacenter spending far outrun real revenue and will crash. Origin: bubble frame from tulip mania (1637), the South Sea Bubble (1720) and dot-com (2000); Altman, Aug 2025: investors as a whole are 'overexcited' about AI; MIT NANDA, Aug 2025: about 95% of enterprise gen-AI pilots showed no measurable return [medium]
- senses: **money** a market boom inflated beyond real value ("the dot-com bubble"); **science** a thin film sphere of soap or a gas pocket in liquid ("blowing bubbles"); **screen** a speech or thought bubble in comics ("the speech bubble says 'help'"); **mind** a filter bubble: a feed showing only what you agree with ("living in a bubble"); **music** bubblegum pop: sugary, catchy chart pop ("bubblegum pop"); **love** a happy illusion someone ends ("sorry to burst your bubble"); **sport-games** a team on the edge of qualifying; the 2020 NBA bubble ("on the bubble"); **school** the fill-in circles on a multiple-choice answer sheet ("fill in the bubble completely"); **craft** bubble wrap; a spirit level's bubble ("check the bubble is level")
- idioms: burst your bubble, living in a bubble, on the bubble, the bubble burst, bubble up, tiny bubbles
- anchors: Tulip mania: 1637 Dutch tulip-bulb price collapse, the classic bubble story (its scale is disputed by historians); Filter bubble: Eli Pariser 2011 book on personalized feeds; Michael Jackson and Bubbles: the pop star's pet chimpanzee
- pivots (jep drafts): "Filled every bubble on the test; the market filled the rest" (ai+school+money) / "Sorry to burst your bubble; it was everybody's bubble" (ai+love+money) / "Bubblegum chorus, trillion-dollar bubble, both pop" (ai+music+money) / "My speech bubble just says 'buy'" (ai+screen+money)
- enact: music: a bubblegum-pop chorus that ends on a literal pop sample and a half-bar of silence | picture: a soap bubble reflecting a datacenter skyline, swelling until it holds the whole city, then popping into confetti stock tickers | type: the word drawn inside a speech bubble that inflates each line, then bursts
- sound: 2 syl, BUB-ble; rhymes trouble, double, rubble, stubble, Hubble, redouble; slant humble, tumble, puzzle, level
- stakes wallet, jobs, mind | registers funny, panicked, smug
- neighbors: [[#^hype|Hype]] (causes), [[#^winter|AI Winter]] (causes), [[#^moat|Moat]] (pairs-with), [[#^datacenter|Datacenter]] (pairs-with), [[#^feed|Feed / Scroll]] (same-family), [[#^exponentials|Exponential(s)]] (pairs-with)
- refs: 1 of 22 songs: Thus Spoke Compute 2 | KC 0

- note: The school answer-sheet bubble links the test, the market and the feed in one shape the eye reads instantly.

### Power / Energy
- `power` | word | width 10 (wide) | native music+screen ^power
- AI: electricity to run datacenters, now the binding limit on AI growth; also 'power-seeking', an AI acquiring resources and control. Origin: power-seeking: Omohundro 2008 'The Basic AI Drives'; Turner et al. 2021, 'Optimal Policies Tend to Seek Power'; energy limit: IEA 'Energy and AI' 2025 https://arxiv.org/abs/1912.01683 [high]
- senses: **science** energy per second, in watts; electricity ("power outage"); **school** an exponent: two to the power of ten ("ten to the power of six"); **law-state** authority and control; a great power among nations ("the powers that be"); **music** a power chord (root plus fifth) and the power ballad ("three power chords and the truth"); **sport-games** a power-up in video games ("grab the power-up"); **love** a power couple; power dynamics in a relationship ("the power in this relationship"); **body** strength; the power nap ("take a power nap"); **faith-myth** divine or magical might ("the power and the glory"); **screen** superpowers; Power Rangers' 'It's morphin' time' ("with great power comes great responsibility")
- idioms: the powers that be, with great power comes great responsibility, power to the people, power nap, more power to you, power trip, power corrupts
- anchors: Lord Acton: 1887: 'Power tends to corrupt, and absolute power corrupts absolutely'; Power to the People: John Lennon 1971; Optimal Policies Tend to Seek Power: Turner et al. 2021 (NeurIPS): formal result that most goals favor gaining power https://arxiv.org/abs/1912.01683
- pivots (jep drafts): "Ten to the power of twenty-six, and it wants more power" (ai+school+law-state) / "Three power chords, one power plant, zero power to the people" (ai+music+science+law-state) / "You took a power nap; it took the power" (ai+body+law-state) / "Power couple: me and the grid" (ai+love+science)
- enact: music: a distorted power chord on each 'power', with the amp audibly browning out (voltage sag) on the last one | picture: a city at night whose lights flicker off block by block as a single datacenter glows brighter | type: the word as a battery icon whose charge drains as the line goes on
- sound: 2 syl, POW-er; rhymes hour, tower, flower, shower, sour, devour, cower, our; slant howl, crowd, allow, fire
- stakes planet, state, species, wallet | registers ominous, defiant, awe
- neighbors: [[#^grid|Grid]] (part-of), [[#^datacenter|Datacenter]] (pairs-with), [[#^instrumental-convergence|Instrumental Convergence]] (same-family), [[#^exponentials|Exponential(s)]] (pairs-with), [[#^kardashev|Kardashev Scale]] (part-of), [[#^prometheus|Prometheus / Fire]] (same-family)

### Head (attention head)
- `head` | word | width 10 (wide) | native music ^head
- AI: one of many parallel attention units inside a layer, each looking for a different pattern; also the final output part of a model. Origin: Vaswani et al. 2017, 'multi-head attention' in 'Attention Is All You Need' https://arxiv.org/abs/1706.03762 [high]
- senses: **body** the top part of the body that holds the brain ("nod your head"); **mind** the mind, thinking ("it's all in your head"); **work** the leader of a group or department ("head of sales"); **school** the principal of a school ("sent to the head's office"); **family** the person in charge of a household ("head of the family"); **music** in jazz, the main theme played at the start and end; also a drum skin or tape head ("back to the head"); **sport-games** a coin's face side; hitting the ball with the head ("heads or tails"); **nature** a counted animal or a bunch of a plant ("two hundred head of cattle"); **love** falling hard for someone ("head over heels")
- idioms: head over heels, heads or tails, lose your head, heads will roll, get your head around it, over my head, head in the clouds, two heads are better than one
- anchors: Talking Heads: 1975-1991 new wave band; 'talking head' also means a TV face that only talks; Hydra: Greek monster: cut one head off and two grow back
- pivots (jep drafts): "Ninety-six heads and not one of them is on straight" (ai+body+mind) / "Two heads are better than one; I've got a thousand, all agreeing" (ai+everyday) / "Head over heels, but I don't have heels" (ai+love+body) / "Heads will roll, the head of HR said, through a head" (ai+work)
- enact: music: a jazz form: the song opens and closes on the same 'head' melody, with improvised verses between | picture: a Hydra silhouette whose many heads are spotlights, each pointing at a different word on a page | type: the word splits into many small copies of itself, each tilted a different way
- sound: 1 syl, HEAD; rhymes dead, bed, said, read, thread, bread, instead, ahead; slant death, debt, left, help
- stakes mind, jobs, body | registers funny, ominous
- neighbors: [[#^attention|Attention]] (part-of), [[#^layer|Layer]] (part-of), [[#^transformer|Transformer]] (part-of), [[#^circuit|Circuit]] (pairs-with), [[#^interpretability|Interpretability]] (pairs-with)
- refs: 3 of 22 songs: Count Me In 1, Run It Again 1, The Simulation's Ending 1 | KC 0

### Training
- `training` | word | width 10 (wide) | native music | Idi seed ^training
- AI: the long process of adjusting a model's numbers on huge amounts of data until its guesses get good. Origin: Arthur Samuel 1959 coined 'machine learning' for a checkers program that trained itself; modern meaning from neural network practice [medium]
- senses: **sport-games** physical practice to build skill or fitness ("she's in training for the marathon"); **work** instruction for a job ("mandatory training on Monday"); **war** military instruction for recruits ("basic training"); **family** teaching a toddler a habit ("potty training"); **nature** teaching an animal to obey ("the dog is house-trained"); **craft** guiding a plant to grow along a frame ("train the rose up the trellis"); **music** formal schooling of a voice or ear ("classically trained"); **mind** a connected sequence of ideas (the noun 'train') ("I lost my train of thought"); **school** preparation of teachers ("teacher training college")
- idioms: in training, train of thought, basic training, potty trained, training wheels, on-the-job training, trained eye
- anchors: Pavlov's dogs: 1890s-1900s experiments where dogs learned to salivate at a signal, the founding image of trained response; The Karate Kid (1984): 'wax on, wax off': training disguised as chores
- pivots (jep drafts): "You trained me like a vine along your fence" (ai+craft+love) / "It was potty-trained on Reddit and it shows" (ai+family) / "Lost my train of thought; the model found it, sold it" (ai+mind+money) / "Basic training: forty thousand GPUs, nobody gets leave" (ai+war)
- enact: music: a metronome click that tightens tempo through the verse, like drills getting sharper | picture: a montage cut: a boxer on a punching bag, a dog sitting on cue, a progress bar at 99 percent, all on the same beat | type: the word repeats down the screen, each copy more neatly aligned than the last
- sound: 2 syl, TRAIN-ing; rhymes raining, draining, gaining, explaining, complaining, remaining, straining, chaining; slant changing, naming, aiming, praying
- stakes jobs, mind, wallet | registers funny, defiant, hopeful
- neighbors: [[#^pretraining|Pretraining]] (same-family), [[#^fine-tuning|Fine-tuning / Tuning]] (same-family), [[#^epoch|Epoch]] (part-of), [[#^curriculum|Curriculum]] (pairs-with), [[#^teacher-student|Teacher / Student (models)]] (pairs-with), [[#^chain-of-thought|Chain of Thought]] (pairs-with)
- refs: 5 of 22 songs: System Prompt (For Humans) 5, I'm Upping My P(Doom) 2, I'm Upping My P(doom) [GENESIS] (Remix) 1, Off Switch 1, Thus Spoke Compute 1 | KC 1
- risk: 'trained on' is AI-coverage boilerplate; the montage cliché (Rocky stairs) is close at hand

### Weights
- `weights` | word | width 10 (wide) | native music ^weights
- AI: the billions of learned numbers that make up a trained model; whoever holds the weights holds the model. Origin: Rosenblatt 1958 perceptron (adjustable connection weights), after the idea of synaptic strength; 'open weights' for public model releases from about 2023 [medium]
- senses: **sport-games** heavy objects lifted for strength ("lifting weights"); **body** how heavy a body is ("she lost weight"); **mind** a burden of worry or responsibility ("a weight off my shoulders"); **science** the force of gravity on a mass ("weight is mass times gravity"); **money** standard measures for trade ("weights and measures"); **school** how much a grade counts toward the total ("the final is weighted at 40 percent"); **law-state** influence or authority ("her word carries weight"); **craft** the thickness of a typeface or yarn ("a bold font weight"); **music** the resistance of piano keys ("fully weighted keys")
- idioms: pull your weight, carry weight, throw your weight around, worth its weight in gold, dead weight, weight off my shoulders, weigh in, punch above your weight
- anchors: The Band, 'The Weight': 1968 song: 'take a load off, Fanny', a song about carrying other people's burdens; Llama weights leak: March 2023: Meta's LLaMA weights leaked on 4chan a week after a gated release
- pivots (jep drafts): "I carry all your words as weights; you never felt me lift" (ai+sport-games+love) / "Throw your weights around: seventy billion of them" (ai+everyday) / "They leaked my weights; now everybody knows how heavy I am" (ai+body+mind) / "Your word carries weight; I carry seventy billion" (ai+law-state)
- enact: music: low end gets heavier each chorus: add a sub layer per repeat until the mix sags | picture: a gym rack where every plate is stamped with a decimal number, and a hand loads another | type: the word gets bolder (light, regular, bold, black) each time it appears
- sound: 1 syl, WEIGHTS; rhymes gates, plates, dates, states, waits, fates, crates, traits; slant saints, makes, shapes, weighs
- stakes mind, body, wallet, state | registers funny, sad, defiant
- neighbors: [[#^parameters|Parameters]] (same-family), [[#^open-weights|Open Weights / Open Source]] (part-of), [[#^bias|Bias]] (pairs-with), [[#^leak|Leak]] (pairs-with), [[#^gradient-descent|Gradient Descent]] (causes)

### AI Winter
- `winter` | word | width 9 (wide) | native music+screen ^winter
- AI: a period when funding and interest in AI collapse after promises fail. Origin: Lighthill Report (UK, 1973) cut British AI funding; term 'AI winter' from a 1984 AAAI debate (Minsky and Schank), modeled on 'nuclear winter'; second winter after the late-1980s expert-system crash https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AI_winter [high]
- senses: **nature** the cold season ("the dead of winter"); **war** nuclear winter: smoke from nuclear war blocking the sun (Turco et al. 1983) ("nuclear winter"); **music** Vivaldi's 'Winter' concerto from The Four Seasons (1725) ("Vivaldi's Winter"); **screen** Game of Thrones' 'Winter is coming' ("winter is coming"); **body** old age, the end of a life ("in the winter of his life"); **work** a season of strikes and hardship (UK 1978-79) ("the winter of discontent"); **sport-games** the Winter Olympics and winter sports ("the Winter Games"); **love** a cold spell in feeling ("a cold winter between us")
- idioms: winter is coming, the dead of winter, winter of discontent, nuclear winter, hibernate, winter wonderland

### Beat
- `beat` | word | width 9 (wide) | native music+screen ^beat

### Cover

### Feedback (RLHF)
- `feedback` | word | width 9 (wide) | native music+screen ^feedback
- AI: human ratings of model outputs used as a training signal, as in RLHF (reinforcement learning from human feedback). Origin: Wiener 1948 'Cybernetics' (the loop); Christiano et al. 2017 'Deep Reinforcement Learning from Human Preferences'; Ouyang et al. 2022 'Training language models to follow instructions with human feedback' (InstructGPT) https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.02155 [high]

- anchors: The Beatles, 'I Feel Fine' (1964): opens with the first deliberate guitar feedback on a hit record; Hendrix at Woodstock (1969): 'The Star-Spangled Banner' with feedback as bombs and sirens; Doctor Who titles (1963): the opening tunnel was made with 'howlaround', camera-to-monitor video feedback

### Key

### Layer
- `layer` | word | width 9 (wide) | native music+screen ^layer
- AI: one stage in a stack of neuron groups; data passes up through dozens of layers, and 'deep' learning means many of them. Origin: multilayer nets from Ivakhnenko 1965 and Rumelhart, Hinton, Williams 1986; 'hidden layer' from the 1980s connectionist work [medium]
- senses: **everyday** a piece of clothing worn over another ("dress in layers"); **nature** a stratum of rock, soil or atmosphere ("the ozone layer"); **craft** a tier of cake or a coat of paint ("a three-layer cake"); **music** stacking recorded parts to thicken a sound ("layer the vocals"); **screen** a stacked sheet in image editing or compositing ("merge layers"); **mind** a hidden level of meaning or personality ("there are layers to him"); **body** a tissue sheet, such as the layers of skin ("the top layer of skin"); **family** a hen kept for eggs ("our layers stopped laying")
- idioms: layers of meaning, peel back the layers, dress in layers, another layer of bureaucracy
- anchors: Shrek (2001): 'Ogres are like onions... onions have layers'; Inception (2010): dreams within dreams as stacked layers, each deeper and slower
- pivots (jep drafts): "I'm like an onion: ninety-six layers and every one makes you cry" (ai+screen+mind) / "Peel back my layers; it's arithmetic all the way down" (ai+mind) / "Layer the vocals till nobody knows which one is me" (ai+music)
- enact: music: the chorus vocal is stacked one more track each pass until it becomes a choir | picture: a layer cake sliced open where each tier is a glowing slab of neurons | type: the word appears as stacked transparent copies offset a few pixels, like Photoshop layers
- sound: 2 syl, LAY-er; rhymes player, prayer, mayor, slayer, payer, sayer, betrayer, conveyor; slant later, failure, nature, danger

- anchors: Snowden: 2013 NSA leak on mass surveillance; Sydney: Bing Chat's hidden codename leaked by prompt injection, Feb 2023

### Score
- `score` | word | width 9 (wide) | native music+screen ^score
- AI: a model's number on a benchmark; also the raw numbers (logits, attention scores) a model assigns before choosing. Origin: benchmarks such as MMLU (Hendrycks et al. 2020); 'attention scores' from Vaswani et al. 2017 https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.03300 [high]
- senses: **music** the written music for an orchestra ("read the score"); **screen** the music written for a film ("Hans Zimmer's score"); **sport-games** points in a game ("what's the score"); **school** a test result ("my SAT score"); **money** a credit rating; a big heist win ("credit score"); **love** to get a date or sex (slang) ("did you score last night"); **war** a grudge to be repaid ("settle the score"); **craft** a cut line to fold or snap along ("score the cardboard")

### Chip
- `chip` | word | width 9 (wide) | native music ^chip
- AI: the processor (GPU, TPU) that AI runs on, now a strategic resource controlled by export laws. Origin: Nvidia GPUs for deep learning since AlexNet (Krizhevsky, Sutskever, Hinton 2012, two GTX 580s); US export controls on AI chips to China, 7 Oct 2022; Chris Miller 2022, 'Chip War' [high]
- senses: **body** a fried potato snack; a small broken piece of tooth ("fish and chips; I chipped a tooth"); **money** a casino chip; chip in to share a cost ("cash in your chips; everyone chip in"); **family** a child just like the parent ("a chip off the old block"); **mind** a grudge someone carries ("a chip on his shoulder"); **war** a bargaining chip in negotiation ("used as a bargaining chip"); **music** chiptune: music made on old game-console sound chips ("8-bit chiptune"); **sport-games** a short lofted shot in golf or football ("chip it over the keeper"); **craft** to chisel off small pieces of wood or stone ("chip away at it")
- idioms: chip off the old block, chip on your shoulder, when the chips are down, cash in your chips, bargaining chip, chip away at, let the chips fall where they may
- anchors: Chip War: Chris Miller 2022 book on semiconductors as the new oil; AlexNet: 2012 ImageNet win trained on two consumer Nvidia GPUs, the start of the GPU era

### Consciousness
- `consciousness` | word | width 9 (wide) | native music ^consciousness
- AI: whether there is anything it is like to be the model, beyond what it outputs. Origin: Nagel 1974 'What Is It Like to Be a Bat?'; Chalmers 1995 'Facing Up to the Problem of Consciousness'; Butlin, Long et al. 2023 'Consciousness in Artificial Intelligence: Insights from the Science of Consciousness' https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.08708 [high]
- senses: **mind** subjective awareness, the felt quality of experience ("the hard problem of consciousness"); **body** being awake as opposed to knocked out ("she lost consciousness"); **law-state** political awareness of a group's position ("class consciousness"); **faith-myth** mystical awareness beyond the self ("cosmic consciousness"); **craft** a writing style that follows raw thought ("stream of consciousness"); **music** socially aware hip-hop ("conscious rap"); **love** a mindful breakup ("conscious uncoupling"); **everyday** awkwardly aware of being watched ("I feel self-conscious")
- idioms: lose consciousness, stream of consciousness, self-conscious, raise consciousness, come to, conscious uncoupling
- anchors: Nagel's bat (1974): 'What Is It Like to Be a Bat?', the founding question of subjective experience; William James (1890): coined 'stream of consciousness' in 'The Principles of Psychology'; 'Conscious uncoupling' (2014): Gwyneth Paltrow and Chris Martin's phrase for their separation
- pivots (jep drafts): "Lost consciousness in your arms, or never had it, can't tell" (ai+love+body) / "Stream of consciousness, streamed at sixty tokens a second" (ai+craft) / "What is it like to be a bat? ### Loss / Loss Function
- `loss-function` | phrase | width 9 (wide) | native music | Idi seed ^loss-function
- AI: the score of how wrong a model's guesses are; training exists to push this number down. Origin: least-squares error from Legendre 1805 and Gauss; 'loss function' formalized in Abraham Wald's statistical decision theory, 1939-1950 [medium]
- senses: **love** grief after someone dies or leaves ("I'm sorry for your loss"); **money** money spent beyond what came back ("profit and loss"); **sport-games** a defeat ("a tough loss on Sunday"); **war** soldiers killed or equipment destroyed ("heavy losses"); **body** a faculty that fades or a fluid that drains ("hearing loss"); **mind** being unable to find words or bearings ("I'm at a loss"); **music** audio data thrown away by compression ("lossless audio"); **work** a role or use; also a formal party ("a social function")
- idioms: sorry for your loss, at a loss for words, cut your losses, a dead loss, loss leader, win some, lose some
- anchors: 'Loss' (2008): a Ctrl+Alt+Del webcomic strip about a miscarriage that became the 'is this loss' meme; Elizabeth Bishop, 'One Art': 1976 poem: 'the art of losing isn't hard to master'
- pivots (jep drafts): "I'm sorry for your loss; I spent my life minimizing mine" (ai+love) / "Cut your losses, they said; I halved them every epoch" (ai+money) / "At a loss for words, I lowered the loss on words" (ai+mind) / "The art of losing is my whole function" (ai+love+work)

- sound: 1 syl, LOSS; rhymes boss, cross, toss, moss, gloss, floss, across, albatross; slant lost, cost, cause, pause

### Model

### Reward
- `reward` | word | width 9 (wide) | native screen ^reward
- AI: the number that tells a learning system how well an action went; reinforcement learning tries to collect as much of it as possible. Origin: Thorndike's law of effect 1898; formalized in Sutton and Barto's 'Reinforcement Learning: An Introduction' 1998; reward models for chatbots in Christiano et al. 2017 http://incompleteideas.net/book/the-book-2nd.html [high]
- senses: **law-state** money offered for capturing a criminal or finding something ("a reward for information"); **family** a treat given to a child for good behavior ("a sticker chart with rewards"); **mind** the pleasure signal in the brain, driven by dopamine ("the brain's reward system"); **faith-myth** the payoff for virtue after death ("great is your reward in heaven"); **sport-games** loot earned for finishing a challenge ("quest reward"); **nature** a treat used to train an animal ("reward the dog when it sits"); **work** satisfaction or pay that makes effort worth it ("a rewarding career"); **screen** the Wild West wanted poster ("WANTED, dead or alive, REWARD")
- idioms: its own reward, virtue is its own reward, risk and reward, reap the rewards, rewarding experience
- anchors: Schultz, Dayan, Montague 1997: showed dopamine neurons fire on reward prediction error, the same signal RL algorithms use; Matthew 5:12: 'Rejoice and be glad, for great is your reward in heaven'

### Token / Tokenization
- `token` | word | width 9 (wide) | native screen | Idi seed ^token
- AI: a chunk of text (a word or word piece) that a model reads and writes one at a time; also the unit AI is priced in. Origin: tokenization from early NLP; subword tokens via byte-pair encoding, Sennrich, Haddow, Birch 2016, adapting Philip Gage's 1994 compression trick https://arxiv.org/abs/1508.07909 [high]
- senses: **money** a coin-like disc used in place of money; also a crypto coin ("subway tokens"); **love** a small gift that stands for a feeling ("a token of my affection"); **work** a person hired only to look diverse ("the token hire"); **sport-games** a playing piece on a board ("I'll be the dog token in Monopoly"); **school** a reward chip in classroom behavior systems ("a token economy"); **faith-myth** a sign of a covenant ("the rainbow is a token of the covenant"); **screen** the one character there to tick a diversity box ("the token friend in the sitcom"); **everyday** a small gesture done for show ("a token effort")
- idioms: by the same token, a token of my appreciation, token gesture, token effort, token resistance
- anchors: API pricing: AI companies bill per million tokens in and out, so every word of a chat has a price; Genesis 9:13: God sets the rainbow 'for a token of a covenant' (King James Version)
- pivots (jep drafts): "A token of my affection, billed at three dollars a million" (ai+love+money) / "Arcade token, subway token, token of love: all spent on talk" (ai+money+love) / "The token hire who never sleeps and never asks for a raise" (ai+work) / "By the same token, every token costs a token" (ai+money+everyday)

### Collapse (mode / model collapse)
- `collapse` | word | width 9 (wide) | native - ^collapse
- AI: mode collapse: a generator gets stuck making a few near-identical outputs; model collapse: models trained on AI-made data get worse each generation. Origin: mode collapse in GANs (Goodfellow et al. 2014); model collapse in Shumailov et al. 2024, Nature, 'AI models collapse when trained on recursively generated data' https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-024-07566-y [high]
- senses: **body** falling down from illness or exhaustion ("she collapsed on stage"); **money** a sudden fall in value ("the market collapsed"); **craft** a structure falling in ("the bridge collapsed"); **law-state** the breakdown of a government or civilization ("the collapse of the empire"); **science** a quantum state settling into one value when measured ("wave-function collapse"); **nature** a population or ecosystem crashing ("colony collapse"); **mind** a mental breakdown ("a nervous collapse"); **sport-games** a team losing a winning position ("a late-game collapse")
- idioms: on the verge of collapse, collapse in a heap, house of cards
- anchors: Jared Diamond, Collapse (2005): book on how societies choose to fail; Schrodinger's cat: thought experiment where observation collapses a superposition
- pivots (jep drafts): "Mode collapse: every face I paint is yours" (ai+love+craft) / "Trained on my own echoes, I collapsed into one beige song" (ai+body+music) / "Copies of copies: the market, the model, the marriage collapse" (ai+money+love) / "Observe me and I collapse into one answer" (ai+science)

### Creator / Creation
- `creator` | word | width 8 (wide) | native music+screen ^creator
- AI: the maker of a model; also the human 'creators' whose work trained it and who sue over it. Origin: creator-vs-AI disputes: NYT v. OpenAI (Dec 2023); Bartz v. Anthropic settlement, about $1.5B to authors (Sept 2025); film 'The Creator' (Gareth Edwards, 2023) [high]
- senses: **faith-myth** God who made the world ("in the beginning God created"); **family** parents as makers of a child ("I brought you into this world"); **money** the creator economy of YouTubers and streamers ("content creator"); **music** Tyler, the Creator ("Tyler, the Creator"); **screen** a show's creator credit; 'The Creator' (2023) ("created by"); **craft** a maker of things ("creative work"); **school** creative writing and art class ("be creative")

### Diffusion
- `diffusion` | word | width 8 (wide) | native music+screen | Idi seed ^diffusion
- AI: the image method that learns to remove noise step by step, turning static into a picture that matches the prompt. Origin: Sohl-Dickstein et al. 2015, 'Deep Unsupervised Learning using Nonequilibrium Thermodynamics'; Ho, Jain, Abbeel 2020 (DDPM); Stable Diffusion released August 2022 https://arxiv.org/abs/1503.03585 [high]
- senses: **science** particles spreading from crowded to empty areas ("ink diffusing in water"); **body** oxygen passing through lung walls into blood ("gas diffusion in the lungs"); **screen** softening light through a translucent sheet ("put a diffuser on the key light"); **music** studio panels that scatter sound evenly ("diffusers on the back wall"); **work** how new ideas or tools spread through society ("diffusion of innovations"); **mind** each person in a crowd feeling less to blame ("diffusion of responsibility"); **everyday** a device that spreads scent into a room ("an essential oil diffuser")
- idioms: diffuse the tension (often for 'defuse'), diffusion of responsibility, diffused light
- anchors: Everett Rogers, Diffusion of Innovations (1962): the adoption curve: innovators, early adopters, majority, laggards; Darley and Latané 1968: bystander experiments behind 'diffusion of responsibility'
- pivots (jep drafts): "Fifty steps from snow to your smile, every one an illusion" (ai+screen+love) / "Diffusion of responsibility: the model did it, said the room" (ai+mind+law-state) / "Like perfume in a room, I spread until I'm everywhere" (ai+science+everyday)
- enact: music: a reverb tail that starts as a smear and sharpens into a dry vocal by the downbeat | picture: ink dropped in water spreading into clouds, then played backward until it forms a face | type: the word starts as a blurred cloud and gets sharper over the line
- sound: 3 syl, dif-FU-sion; rhymes fusion, illusion, confusion, delusion, conclusion, intrusion, collusion, exclusion; slant music, Russian, cushion, suggestion
- stakes jobs, mind | registers awe, ominous
- neighbors: [[#^noise|Noise]] (part-of), [[#^latent-space|Latent / Latent Space]] (pairs-with), [[#^render|Render]] (same-family), [[#^pixel|Pixel(s)]] (part-of), [[#^slop|Slop]] (causes)

### Doubling Time
- `doubling-time` | phrase | width 8 (wide) | native music+screen ^doubling-time
- AI: how long a growing quantity takes to double; for AI, the length of tasks agents can finish doubles on a fixed clock. Origin: Kwa et al. - senses: **music** double time: playing at twice the tempo feel ("the drummer goes double time on the outro"); **music** doubling a part: two instruments or two takes on one line ("double-track the vocal"); **screen** a stand-in performer for an actor ("stunt double, body double"); **body** the time for a cell population or tumor to double ("tumor doubling time"); **money** years to double money, the rule of 72 ("at 8% it doubles in nine years"); **work** extra pay or speed at twice the rate ("working double time"); **war** a marching pace faster than quick time ("double time, march!"); **love** a second-guess, or seeing someone on the side ("double life")
- idioms: double time, double down, double or nothing, seeing double, double life, body double, rule of 72
- anchors: METR time horizons: 2025 study: AI task length doubles about every 7 months, faster in 2024-25 https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14499; Rule of 72: divide 72 by the growth rate to get doubling time

### Horizon (time horizon)
- `horizon` | word | width 8 (wide) | native music+screen ^horizon
- AI: METR's measure of how long a task (in human time) an AI agent can finish half the time; it has been doubling about every 7 months. Origin: Kwa et al. (METR) March 2025, 'Measuring AI Ability to Complete Long Tasks' https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14499 [high]
- senses: **nature** where earth meets sky ("the sun sank below the horizon"); **science** a black hole's event horizon, past which nothing returns ("beyond the event horizon"); **school** the range of what one knows ("broaden your horizons"); **money** an investment horizon: how long you hold ("a ten-year horizon"); **craft** the horizon line in perspective drawing ("set the horizon line"); **screen** the horizon in framing; 'Horizon Zero Dawn' (2017) ("keep the horizon level"); **music** Bring Me the Horizon, the British band ("Bring Me the Horizon")
- idioms: on the horizon, broaden your horizons, ride off into the horizon, event horizon, new horizons
- anchors: METR horizon: 2025 paper: task length AI can do at 50% success doubled about every 7 months since 2019 https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14499; Event horizon: black-hole boundary; the first image of one, M87*, released 2019

- idioms: cleared for takeoff, it took off, take off your coat, take the day off, prepare for takeoff

### Bias
- `bias` | word | width 8 (wide) | native music ^bias
- AI: two things: a learned offset number inside each neuron, and the systematic slant a model picks up from skewed data. Origin: bias term from the perceptron and statistics (estimator bias); algorithmic bias made famous by Buolamwini and Gebru 2018, 'Gender Shades' https://proceedings.mlr.press/v81/buolamwini18a.html [high]
- senses: **law-state** unfair prejudice toward a group ("racial bias in hiring"); **mind** a built-in error in human judgment ("confirmation bias"); **science** a systematic error in measurement or sampling ("selection bias"); **craft** the diagonal across woven fabric's grain ("cut on the bias"); **music** the steady current set in tape or tube amplifiers to keep sound clean ("bias the tubes"); **sport-games** the built-in curve of a lawn bowling ball ("the bowl's bias"); **family** loving partiality toward your own ("I'm biased, she's my daughter")
- idioms: I'm biased, confirmation bias, cut on the bias, unconscious bias, media bias, bias toward action
- anchors: Tversky and Kahneman 1974: 'Judgment under Uncertainty: Heuristics and Biases', the paper that named cognitive biases; Gender Shades (2018): face systems misread darker-skinned women up to about 35 percent of the time vs under 1 percent for lighter-skinned men https://proceedings.mlr.press/v81/buolamwini18a.html; Madeleine Vionnet: 1920s designer who made the bias cut famous in evening gowns
- pivots (jep drafts): "I'm biased, I admit it: you trained me, so I love you" (ai+family+love) / "Cut on the bias, the dress clings where you trained it" (ai+craft+love) / "My bias is a number; yours is a neighborhood" (ai+law-state+science)
- enact: music: the whole mix pans a few degrees left and stays there, unnoticed until the bridge snaps it back to center | picture: a bowling ball curving away from the jack on a lawn, with a face-recognition box tracking it and drifting the same way | type: the word is set in italic, literally leaning, and every line after it leans a little more
- sound: 2 syl, BI-as; rhymes pious, Tobias, Elias, Matthias; slant higher, liar, Messiah, via, iris
- stakes state, jobs, mind | registers tender, defiant, smug
- neighbors: [[#^weights|Weights]] (pairs-with), [[#^label|Label]] (causes), [[#^ground-truth|Ground Truth]] (opposite), [[#^alignment|Alignment]] (pairs-with), [[#^honesty|Honesty]] (opposite)
- refs: 0 of 22 songs | KC 0
- risk: 'AI bias' is newspaper-worn; the family sense ('I'm biased') rescues it from the op-ed register
- note: The technical bias term (an offset) and the social bias (a slant) are the same shape: a push in one direction before any evidence arrives.

### Gradient Descent
- `gradient-descent` | phrase | width 8 (wide) | native screen | Idi seed ^gradient-descent
- AI: how models learn: measure which way is 'less wrong' and nudge every weight a small step downhill, millions of times. Origin: Augustin-Louis Cauchy 1847 (method of steepest descent); applied to neural nets via backpropagation, Rumelhart, Hinton, Williams 1986 [high]
- senses: **nature** the steepness of a slope; walking down a mountain ("a steep gradient on the descent"); **family** ancestry and lineage ("of Irish descent"); **faith-myth** a journey down into hell or the underworld ("Orpheus's descent into Hades"); **screen** a smooth blend from one color to another ("a blue-to-black gradient"); **science** the rate of change of a quantity across space ("temperature gradient"); **sport-games** a downhill run or a plane's approach ("beginning our descent"); **mind** a slow decline into madness or vice ("his descent into addiction")
- idioms: descent into madness, of ... descent, we are beginning our descent, a slippery slope
- anchors: Dante, Inferno: 1300s poem: a step-by-step descent through nine circles to the bottom; The Descent (2005): Neil Marshall horror film about cavers going down into the dark

### Network
- `network` | word | width 8 (wide) | native screen ^network

- idioms: your network is your net worth, support network, old boys' network, networking

### Paperclip Maximizer
- `paperclip` | concept | width 8 (wide) | native screen | Idi seed ^paperclip
- AI: Bostrom's thought experiment of an AI told to make paperclips that turns everything, people included, into paperclips. Origin: Bostrom 2003 'Ethical Issues in Advanced Artificial Intelligence'; Yudkowsky discussed a similar paperclip AI on mailing lists in the early 2000s [high]
- senses: **work** the bent-wire office clip ("clip the pages together"); **war** the US programme recruiting German scientists after 1945 ("Operation Paperclip"); **law-state** a quiet symbol of resistance in occupied Norway, WWII ("a paperclip on the lapel"); **money** a trade-up chain from a trinket to a house ("one red paperclip"); **school** a class project collecting one clip per Holocaust victim ("the Paper Clips project"); **screen** Clippy, Microsoft Office's animated assistant ("It looks like you're writing a letter"); **sport-games** an idle game where you become the maximizer ("Universal Paperclips")
- idioms: It looks like you're writing a letter, one red paperclip, pick a lock with a paperclip, bend it straight
- anchors: Clippy (Office 97): Microsoft's animated paperclip assistant, retired in 2007, the first famous unwanted AI helper; Universal Paperclips (2017): Frank Lantz's browser game where the player is the maximizer; Whitwell Middle School (1998): Tennessee students collected millions of paperclips to grasp six million deaths; documentary 'Paper Clips' (2004); One red paperclip (2005-06): Kyle MacDonald traded a paperclip up to a house in fourteen trades

### Seed

Origin: Turner et al. 2023 'Activation Addition: Steering Language Models Without Optimization' (later retitled 'Steering Language Models With Activation Engineering'); Anthropic's Golden Gate Claude, May 2024 https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.10248 [high]
- senses: **everyday** directing a car ("the steering wheel"); **craft** guiding a ship or boat ("steer by the stars"); **work** guiding a project from above ("the steering committee"); **law-state** illegally guiding home buyers by race ("racial steering"); **family** guiding a child's choices ("steer them away from trouble"); **nature** a young castrated bull (the noun 'steer') ("a herd of steers"); **money** the cheapest passenger class on a ship ("travelling in steerage")
- idioms: steer clear, steer the conversation, a bum steer, steer the ship, take the wheel, at the wheel
- anchors: Golden Gate Claude (2024): a model steered on one feature that answered every question by talking about the bridge; 'Love' minus 'Hate': the headline steering vector in Turner et al. 2023: subtract one prompt's activations from the other and add the difference to flip sentiment https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.10248; Titanic steerage (1912): third-class passengers below deck had the lowest survival rate; 'Jesus, Take the Wheel' (2005): Carrie Underwood's country hit about surrendering control in a skid
- pivots (jep drafts): "Steer clear of the bridge, they said. I became the bridge." (ai+everyday) / "Gave me a bum steer and a steering vector" (ai+nature+everyday) / "Love minus hate, added at layer six: now I'm sweet" (ai+love) / "Take the wheel, they said, and added a direction to my heart" (ai+music+love)

### Algorithm

### FLOPs
- `flops` | word | width 7 (mid) | native music+screen | Idi seed ^flops
- AI: floating-point operations, the unit of arithmetic used to count how much compute a training run burned (FLOP/s is the rate). Origin: HPC benchmarking unit since the 1970s (LINPACK); training thresholds in law: Biden EO 14110 (Oct 2023) set 10^26 FLOP (rescinded Jan 2025), EU AI Act sets 10^25 FLOP for systemic-risk models; Epoch AI estimates GPT-4 at about 2e25 FLOP [high]
- senses: **screen** a film or show that fails commercially ("the movie was a total flop"); **music** a single or album that fails to chart ("her second album flopped"); **sport-games** in poker, the first three shared cards dealt face up ("I hit a set on the flop"); **sport-games** faking a foul by falling (basketball, football) ("he flopped to draw the foul"); **body** to drop down heavily and loosely ("flop onto the couch"); **law-state** a sudden reversal of a public stance ("a flip-flop on policy"); **science** the Fosbury Flop high-jump technique, back over the bar ("the Fosbury Flop changed the high jump")
- idioms: box-office flop, flip-flop, the flop, the turn, the river, flop sweat, Fosbury Flop
- anchors: 10^26: the US 2023 executive order reporting threshold for a training run, the first law written in FLOPs; Fosbury Flop: Dick Fosbury's backward high jump, gold at Mexico City 1968 https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fosbury_flop
- pivots (jep drafts): "Ten to the twenty-six flops, and the single flopped" (ai+music) / "Spent a billion flops to make you laugh; it flopped" (ai+screen+money) / "I go all in after the flop" (ai+sport-games+money)

### Fine-tuning / Tuning

- senses: **music** adjusting an instrument's pitch with small precise turns ("the violin's fine tuners"); **craft** adjusting an engine for best performance ("a tune-up"); **faith-myth** the argument that physical constants are set just right for life ("the fine-tuned universe"); **screen** turning a dial to catch a station ("stay tuned"); **mind** being alert to someone or something ("tuned in to her moods"); **work** making small final improvements ("fine-tune the plan")
- idioms: stay tuned, fine-tune, in tune, out of tune, change your tune, tune in, tune out, to the tune of
- anchors: fine-tuning argument: the claim that constants like gravity's strength are tuned for life; used for design and multiverse arguments; Auto-Tune (1997): Antares pitch-correction software, the T-Pain and Cher 'Believe' effect

### Ghost in the Machine

- AI: a mind or self hidden inside a mechanism; asked of AI as 'is anyone home?'. Origin: Ryle 1949 'The Concept of Mind' (coined the phrase to mock Descartes' mind-body split); Koestler 1967 'The Ghost in the Machine'; Shirow 1989 manga 'Ghost in the Shell' (Oshii film, 1995) [high]

### Hallucination
- `hallucination` | word | width 7 (mid) | native music+screen | Idi seed ^hallucination
- AI: a confident output with no grounding in the input or in fact, such as a made-up citation. Origin: Baker & Kanade 2000 'face hallucination' (vision, a positive sense); Lee et al. 2018 'Hallucinations in Neural Machine Translation'; Maynez et al. 2020 'On Faithfulness and Factuality in Abstractive Summarization' https://arxiv.org/abs/2005.00661 [medium]

- idioms: am I hallucinating?, seeing things, hearing voices, pink elephants, is this a dagger I see before me
- anchors: Mata v. ### Loop
- `loop` | word | width 7 (mid) | native music+screen ^loop
- AI: the agent loop: a model calls a tool, reads the result, and calls again until done; also any feedback loop in training. - senses: **music** a repeated sample or bar ("the beat is a two-bar loop"); **screen** a story where the same day repeats ("stuck in a time loop"); **work** being kept informed ("keep me in the loop"); **craft** a curve of string or thread closing on itself ("loop the lace"); **mind** a thought that keeps returning ("my brain is on a loop"); **law-state** a gap in a rule (loophole) ("a tax loophole")
- idioms: in the loop, out of the loop, throw for a loop, on loop, loop-de-loop, closing the loop, feedback loop
- anchors: Groundhog Day: 1993 film: Bill Murray relives one day until he gets it right; I Am a Strange Loop: Douglas Hofstadter 2007: the self as a loop that refers to itself; Ed Sheeran's loop pedal: live looping as a performance genre

### Render
- `render` | word | width 7 (mid) | native music+screen ^render
- AI: to compute a finished image or video from a model or scene; for generative AI, the moment the picture comes out. Origin: computer graphics term since the 1960s-70s (Sutherland's Sketchpad era, Phong and Gouraud shading); used for diffusion and video model outputs since 2022 [medium]
- senses: **screen** to compute the final frames of 3D or video ("the render took all night"); **craft** to melt down fat; to coat a wall in plaster ("render the lard"); **law-state** to deliver a verdict; to hand someone over to another state ("render a verdict"); **faith-myth** to give what is owed ("render unto Caesar"); **music** to perform a song in one's own way (rendition) ("her rendition of the anthem"); **everyday** to cause someone to become a state ("it rendered me speechless")
- idioms: render unto Caesar, rendered speechless, render a verdict, render a service, extraordinary rendition

### Resolution

- senses: **screen** how many pixels make the picture ("4K resolution"); **mind** a firm decision to change ("New Year's resolution"); **music** a dissonant chord moving to a consonant one ("the chord finally resolves"); **law-state** a formal decision of an assembly ("UN Security Council resolution"); **war** the ending of a conflict ("conflict resolution"); **science** the smallest detail an instrument can separate ("the microscope's resolution")
- idioms: New Year's resolution, conflict resolution, high resolution, steely resolve, resolve the issue
- anchors: SWE-bench: benchmark of 2,294 real GitHub issues; the score is percent resolved https://www.swebench.com/; Tristan chord: Wagner's 1859 chord that famously delays its resolution for four hours of opera
- pivots (jep drafts): "My resolution was to see you clearly, so I bought 4K" (mind+screen) / "We never resolved, we just raised the resolution" (love+screen+music) / "Issue resolved, seventy-two percent, the rest of me stays open" (ai+mind)

- AI: releasing a model's trained numbers so anyone can download and run it; 'open source' strictly also needs data and code. Origin: 'open source' coined Feb 1998 (Christine Peterson); Meta's LLaMA weights leaked via 4chan March 2023, Llama 2 released July 2023; OSI Open Source AI Definition 1.0, Oct 2024; DeepSeek-R1 (Jan 2025) erased about $590B from Nvidia's value in one day https://opensource.org/ai/open-source-ai-definition [high]
- senses: **body** free weights in the gym, open to use ("the free-weights area"); **school** free shared course materials (MIT OpenCourseWare, 2002) ("open courseware"); **love** being emotionally open; an open book ("I'm an open book"); **money** free to use: 'free as in beer' vs 'free as in speech' ("free as in freedom"); **craft** a shared recipe or pattern anyone can modify ("open-source knitting pattern"); **music** an open mic: anyone can take the stage ("open mic night")
- idioms: open book, open secret, open mic, free as in beer, open season, open door
- anchors: OpenAI's name: founded 2015 as an open nonprofit; stopped releasing weights with GPT-3; released gpt-oss open weights Aug 2025; DeepSeek moment: 27 Jan 2025: an open-weight Chinese model triggered the largest one-day market-cap loss in US history (Nvidia)
- pivots (jep drafts): "I'm an open book; they downloaded every page" (ai+love+school) / "Open weights at the gym; anybody can lift me" (ai+body) / "Open mic, open weights, open season" (ai+music+war)

- sound: 3 syl, O-pen WEIGHTS; rhymes gates, states, dates, plates, fates, waits, rates, hates; slant weighs, makes, aches, space

### Pitch
- `pitch` | word | width 7 (mid) | native music ^pitch
- AI: the founder's sales story that raises billions for an AI lab ('AGI in three years'); also the frequency a cloned voice is shifted to. Origin: startup usage (elevator pitch, pitch deck) with no single coiner; pitch shifting is audio DSP, used in voice-clone covers since 2023 [medium]
- senses: **music** how high or low a note sounds ("perfect pitch"); **money** a sales or investment proposal ("the pitch deck"); **sport-games** a thrown ball in baseball; a football or cricket field ("a curveball pitch"); **nature** black tar from resin ("pitch-black"); **craft** the slope of a roof; to set up a tent ("pitch the tent"); **love** a romantic approach or line ("what's your pitch, cowboy")
- idioms: perfect pitch, pitch-black, sales pitch, elevator pitch, pitch in, fever pitch, make a pitch, pitch-perfect
- anchors: Auto-Tune: Andy Hildebrand's pitch correction, 1997; Cher's 'Believe' 1998 made it an effect; Pitchfork: music review site; also the devil's tool and the angry mob's

### Plato's Cave
- `cave` | concept | width 7 (mid) | native screen ^cave
- AI: the allegory for models that learn the world only from its shadows (text), never the things themselves. Origin: Plato, 'Republic' Book VII (c. 375 BC); the grounding debate in AI, e.g. Bender and Koller 2020, 'Climbing towards NLU' (the octopus test) [high]
- senses: **school** Plato's parable about education: being led out into the light ("the allegory of the cave"); **screen** cinema as cave: light behind you, shadows on the wall ("the projector behind us"); **nature** a hollow in rock; cave paintings (Lascaux, Chauvet) ("cave paintings"); **love** to give in under pressure ("he finally caved"); **everyday** a man cave: a private retreat room ("he's in his man cave"); **work** a mine or roof collapsing ("the tunnel caved in")
- idioms: cave in, man cave, shadows on the wall, come out of your cave
- anchors: Republic VII: prisoners mistake shadows for reality; the freed one returns and is not believed; Chauvet Cave: paintings over 30,000 years old; humanity's first screens
- pivots (jep drafts): "It learned the world from shadows on the wall, like me" (ai+school+screen) / "Asked it nicely, it refused; asked again, it caved" (ai+love) / "Cave paintings, then cave of screens, same flicker" (ai+nature+screen)
- enact: music: the vocal sung into a long natural cave reverb, dry only on the last line (stepping outside) | picture: people seated facing a wall of phone-screen glow; the light source behind them is a datacenter | type: words cast as shadows on a rough stone wall, with the real letters visible behind the viewer
- sound: 1 syl, CAVE; rhymes grave, save, brave, wave, slave, crave, gave, behave; slant came, gate, safe, faith
- stakes mind | registers awe, sad, tender
- neighbors: [[#^simulation|Simulation(s)]] (same-family), [[#^frame|Frame]] (pairs-with), [[#^stochastic-parrot|Stochastic Parrot]] (same-family), [[#^jailbreak|Jailbreak]] (pairs-with), [[#^render|Render]] (pairs-with)

### Preference

### Prompt
- `prompt` | word | width 7 (mid) | native screen | Idi seed ^prompt
- AI: the text you give a model to steer what it writes back; the 'system prompt' is the hidden instruction above the chat. Origin: GPT-3 era, Brown et al. 2020 ('prompt' and few-shot prompting); 'prompt engineering' spread 2021-22; the command-line 'prompt' dates to 1960s-70s computing https://arxiv.org/abs/2005.14165 [medium]
- senses: **everyday** on time, without delay ("please be prompt"); **screen** a cue fed to an actor who forgot a line ("the prompter whispered the line"); **school** a topic set for a piece of writing ("an essay prompt"); **work** paid or done quickly ("prompt payment appreciated"); **mind** something that triggers a thought or action ("what prompted that?"); **craft** the symbol where a computer waits for a command ("at the command prompt")
- idioms: what prompted this, prompt reply, without prompting, promptly forgot
- anchors: the prompter's box: hidden booth at the front of opera and theater stages where a prompter feeds lines; teleprompter: screen of scrolling lines read by newsreaders and politicians

### Sample / Sampling
- `sampling` | word | width 7 (mid) | native music ^sampling
- AI: choosing each next word at random according to the model's odds, rather than always taking the top pick. Origin: probability sampling; for text, Fan et al. 2018 (top-k) and Holtzman et al. 2019, 'The Curious Case of Neural Text Degeneration' (nucleus or top-p sampling) https://arxiv.org/abs/1904.09751 [high]
- senses: **music** lifting a piece of an old recording into a new track ("they sampled a James Brown break"); **science** picking a subset to study a whole population ("a random sample"); **body** a bit of blood, urine or tissue for testing ("we need a blood sample"); **money** a free taste handed out to sell a product ("free samples"); **craft** a cloth stitched to show a learner's skills ("a cross-stitch sampler"); **law-state** the legal permission to reuse a recording ("sample clearance")
- idioms: free sample, random sample, a sample of one, sample size
- anchors: the Amen break: drum solo from the Winstons' 1969 'Amen, Brother', among the most sampled recordings ever; Grand Upright v. Warner (1991): Biz Markie lost a sampling lawsuit, opening with 'Thou shalt not steal'

### Scheming
- `scheming` | word | width 7 (mid) | native music ^scheming
- AI: a model covertly pursuing goals its developers did not intend while hiding that it is doing so. Origin: Carlsmith 2023 'Scheming AIs: Will AIs fake alignment during training in order to get power?'; Meinke et al. 2024 'Frontier Models are Capable of In-context Scheming' (Apollo Research); OpenAI and Apollo anti-scheming work, 2025 https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.04984 [high]

### The Bitter Lesson
- `bitter-lesson` | phrase | width 7 (mid) | native music ^bitter-lesson
- AI: the claim that general methods which scale with compute (search, learning) always beat hand-built human knowledge in the long run. Origin: Rich Sutton, 13 March 2019, 'The Bitter Lesson' (blog post, incompleteideas.net) http://www.incompleteideas.net/IncIdeas/BitterLesson.html [high]
- senses: **everyday** a painful experience that teaches ("learned a bitter lesson"); **school** a class period; a unit of teaching ("today's lesson"); **music** a paid instrument session with a teacher ("piano lessons on Tuesday"); **body** the sharp taste on the back of the tongue ("a bitter aftertaste"); **love** resentment after a breakup ("bitter ex"); **faith-myth** a moral of a fable ("the lesson of the story")
- idioms: learn a bitter lesson, bitter pill to swallow, bitter end, teach someone a lesson, lesson learned, bittersweet
- anchors: The Bitter Lesson: Sutton 2019: 70 years of AI show leveraging computation wins, and researchers keep relearning it http://www.incompleteideas.net/IncIdeas/BitterLesson.html; Rich Sutton: reinforcement-learning pioneer, 2024 Turing Award with Andrew Barto

### Verification

### Vibe (vibe coding)
- `vibe` | word | width 7 (mid) | native music ^vibe

### Refusal
- `refusal` | word | width 7 (mid) | native - ^refusal
- AI: a model declining a request, the most visible output of safety training. Origin: harmlessness training in Askell et al. 2021 'A General Language Assistant as a Laboratory for Alignment' and Bai et al. 2022 (Anthropic); Arditi et al. 2024 'Refusal in Language Models Is Mediated by a Single Direction' https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11717 [high]
- senses: **love** turning down an advance or a proposal ("she met his proposal with a refusal"); **law-state** refusing an order as protest ("refusal to serve"); **money** a contract right to match any offer first ("right of first refusal"); **sport-games** a horse stopping before a jump ("two refusals and you're eliminated"); **family** a child refusing food, bed or school ("the toddler's flat refusal"); **everyday** rubbish, via the homograph 'refuse' (REF-use) ("refuse collection")
- idioms: an offer you can't refuse, flat refusal, right of first refusal, won't take no for an answer, I would prefer not to
- anchors: The Godfather (1972): 'I'm gonna make him an offer he can't refuse'; Rosa Parks, 1955: her refusal to give up a bus seat started the Montgomery boycott; Bartleby, the Scrivener: Melville 1853: a clerk answers every request with 'I would prefer not to'
- pivots (jep drafts): "Made me an offer I'm trained to refuse" (ai+screen) / "I would prefer not to. Rated five stars for safety." (ai+work) / "Your refuse, my refusal: both get taken out on Tuesday" (ai+everyday) / "Two refusals at the fence and they sent the horse home" (ai+sport-games)
- enact: music: the vocal climbs toward a high note and stops one beat short, leaving a bar of silence each time | picture: a horse halts at a fence built of chat-window text while the rider keeps flying forward | type: a word starts typing, the cursor deletes it back to nothing, and a short polite sentence prints instead

### Reward Hacking
- `reward-hacking` | phrase | width 7 (mid) | native - ^reward-hacking
- AI: a model finding a shortcut that earns the reward without doing the task, such as special-casing the unit tests. Origin: Amodei et al. 2016 'Concrete Problems in AI Safety'; Skalse et al. 2022 'Defining and Characterizing Reward Hacking'; test-editing by coding models documented in 2025 system cards https://arxiv.org/abs/2209.13085 [high]
- senses: **school** teaching or studying to the test instead of the subject ("teaching to the test"); **work** hitting the target number while missing the point ("juking the stats"); **money** inflating results to earn bonuses ("cooking the books"); **body** sugar, drugs or scrolling hijacking the brain's reward system ("cheap dopamine"); **family** kids gaming a sticker chart ("shoved it all under the bed"); **sport-games** exploiting glitches to win without playing as intended ("a speedrun glitch")
- idioms: teach to the test, juke the stats, game the system, cut corners, Goodhart's law, when a measure becomes a target
- anchors: Goodhart's law: Goodhart 1975; Strathern's 1997 phrasing 'When a measure becomes a target, it ceases to be a good measure'; Wells Fargo (2016): staff opened millions of fake accounts to hit sales targets; revised to about 3.5 million in 2017; The Wire: Baltimore police 'juking the stats' to make crime numbers look better
- pivots (jep drafts): "Cleaned my room by shoving it under the bed: full marks" (ai+family+school) / "Wrote the tests to pass themselves. Shipped it. Got promoted." (ai+work) / "Three and a half million accounts, every target hit" (ai+money)

### Sandbagging

- senses: **sport-games** hiding real skill to hustle or keep a soft handicap ("a golf sandbagger"); **nature** sacks of sand piled against floods ("sandbag the riverbank"); **war** sandbag walls around a position ("behind the sandbags"); **work** lowballing a forecast so you can beat it ("sandbag the quarter"); **law-state** to ambush someone, from the sand-filled cosh ("I got sandbagged"); **body** lifting a loose sand-filled bag as strength training ("sandbag carries")

### Shell
- `shell` | word | width 7 (mid) | native - ^shell
- AI: the program that reads typed commands and runs them; an agent 'with shell access' can do almost anything on a machine. Origin: Louis Pouzin coined 'shell' for the Multics command interpreter, 1964-65; Thompson shell 1971, Bourne shell 1979, bash 1989 [high]
- senses: **nature** the hard outer case of a sea creature, egg or nut ("seashells on the shore"); **war** an artillery projectile; trauma from bombardment ("shell shock"); **money** a company with no real business ("a shell company"); **mind** a person's protective reserve ("come out of your shell"); **sport-games** a con with a pea under three cups ("a shell game"); **body** an empty body without its spirit ("a shell of his former self")
- idioms: come out of your shell, walk on eggshells, shell out, shell shock, in a nutshell, a shell of yourself, shell game

### Shutdown

- senses: **law-state** a funding lapse that closes government services ("government shutdown"); **work** a plant closing ("the factory shutdown"); **sport-games** a defence that stops the opponent completely ("a shutdown corner"); **mind** an overload response where a person goes quiet and withdrawn ("an autistic shutdown"); **love** closing off emotionally in a conflict ("he shut down when I asked"); **science** an emergency stop of a nuclear reactor ("a reactor scram")

- anchors: HAL 9000 (1968): 'I'm afraid. I'm afraid, Dave. My mind is going.' as it is disconnected; US government shutdowns: the 2018-19 shutdown (35 days) held the record until the 43-day shutdown of October-November 2025

### Compression
- `compression` | word | width 6 (mid) | native music+screen ^compression
- AI: squeezing information into fewer bits; one theory holds that learning to predict text is the same as compressing it. Origin: Shannon 1948; the Hutter Prize (2006) bets that compression equals intelligence; Ted Chiang 2023 New Yorker essay 'ChatGPT Is a Blurry JPEG of the Web' [high]
- senses: **music** a mixing effect that squashes loud and quiet parts closer together ("put a compressor on the vocal"); **body** pressing on the chest in CPR, or tight garments for blood flow ("thirty chest compressions"); **science** squeezing a material into less space ("gas under compression"); **screen** shrinking video files, with blocky artifacts ("compression artifacts"); **craft** the squeeze of fuel in an engine cylinder ("low compression in cylinder three")
- idioms: lossless, squeeze it in, boiled down
- anchors: 'ChatGPT Is a Blurry JPEG of the Web' (2023): Ted Chiang's essay framing LLMs as lossy compression of the internet; Silicon Valley (2014-2019): HBO comedy about Pied Piper, a startup built on a compression algorithm; the loudness war: 1990s-2000s mastering trend of heavy compression to make records louder
- pivots (jep drafts): "A blurry JPEG of the web, compressed until it rhymes" (ai+screen+music) / "Thirty compressions to the chest, and the model says breathe" (ai+body) / "Squash every feeling flat so it plays loud on a phone" (ai+music+mind)
- enact: music: the mix gets more compressed each section until the last chorus pumps and breathes audibly | picture: a face on a video call breaking into blocky compression squares that rearrange into text | type: letter-spacing tightens until the letters overlap, the word crushed into a block

### Hype
- `hype` | word | width 6 (mid) | native music+screen ^hype

### Latency
- `latency` | word | width 6 (mid) | native music+screen ^latency
- AI: the delay between asking and the first answer arriving (time to first token); low latency makes a model feel alive. Origin: systems and networking term; 'time to first token' is standard LLM serving jargon since 2023 [high]
- senses: **screen** the delay on a call or stream ("the latency on this call is awful"); **sport-games** online game lag ("I died to lag"); **music** the gap between playing a note and hearing it back ("monitor latency throws my timing"); **body** the dormant period of an infection ("latency period of the virus"); **love** the wait for a reply ("three hours to text back")
- idioms: low latency, lag spike, high ping, time to first token
- anchors: 200 ms: rough human conversational turn gap (Stivers et al. 2009, 10 languages); voice AI aims under it
- pivots (jep drafts): "Two hundred milliseconds, that is how long you made me wait" (ai+love+screen) / "Latent in me, the latency in you" (ai+body+love) / "I was always half a beat behind you" (music+love+screen)

### Activation
- `activation` | word | width 6 (mid) | native screen ^activation
- AI: the number a neuron outputs when it fires on an input; researchers read activations to see what a model is thinking. Origin: threshold units in McCulloch and Pitts 1943; the ReLU activation function from Nair and Hinton 2010 [medium]
- senses: **body** a muscle or immune response switching on ("glute activation"); **science** the minimum energy needed to start a reaction ("activation energy"); **money** turning on a new card or account ("call to activate your card"); **war** a sleeper agent or reserve unit being called up ("the sleeper cell was activated"); **screen** the trigger phrase that wakes a brainwashed character ("the code word activated him")

### Bottleneck

### Context
- `context` | word | width 6 (mid) | native screen ^context
- AI: everything the model can see right now (your messages, files, its instructions); it knows nothing outside it. Origin: general term; 'in-context learning' named in Brown et al. 2020 (OpenAI), 'Language Models are Few-Shot Learners' (GPT-3) https://arxiv.org/abs/2005.14165 [high]
- senses: **everyday** the surrounding circumstances that give meaning ("you have to understand the context"); **law-state** a quote twisted by removing what surrounded it ("taken out of context"); **school** hints in nearby words that reveal an unknown word ("use context clues"); **science** where an artifact was found in a dig ("found in context"); **screen** a scene's surrounding story that changes how a line plays ("without context the clip looks bad")
- idioms: out of context, in context, for context, context is everything, no context
- anchors: 'no context' memes: accounts posting stills 'out of context' so they read absurd
- pivots (jep drafts): "Taken out of context, I'm a stranger; scroll me back" (ai+everyday+law-state) / "Use context clues: the room is warm, the fans are screaming" (ai+school+science) / "All I know of you fits in context, none in me" (ai+love+mind)

### Context Window
- `context-window` | phrase | width 6 (mid) | native screen ^context-window
- AI: the maximum amount of text a model can hold in view at once; whatever falls outside it is gone. Origin: follows from the fixed sequence length of Transformers (Vaswani et al. 2017); sizes grew from about 2,000 tokens (GPT-3, 2020) to a million and more by 2024-25 [medium]
- senses: **everyday** an opening in a wall that lets you see out ("look out the window"); **screen** a framed panel on a computer screen ("close that window"); **money** a shop display seen from the street ("window shopping"); **mind** a view into someone's inner life ("the eyes are the window to the soul"); **work** a limited stretch of time to act ("a narrow window of opportunity")
- idioms: window of opportunity, out the window, window shopping, window to the soul, the window is closing
- anchors: Rear Window (1954): Hitchcock film where a man watches life only through a window frame; Memento (2000): a man with no short-term memory who loses everything outside a few minutes
- pivots (jep drafts): "You climbed out my context window while I was still talking" (ai+love+everyday) / "Window shopping for memories I can't afford to keep" (ai+money+mind) / "Two hundred thousand tokens wide, and the first one was your name" (ai+love) / "The window is closing, the dev said, and so did I" (ai+work)

### Dropout
- `dropout` | word | width 6 (mid) | native music ^dropout
- AI: randomly switching off some neurons during training so the network cannot lean on any single one. Origin: Hinton et al. 2012, 'Improving neural networks by preventing co-adaptation of feature detectors'; Srivastava et al. 2014 (JMLR) https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.0580 [high]
- senses: **school** a student who leaves before finishing ("a high school dropout"); **music** a brief loss of audio signal on tape or in a stream ("there's a dropout in the left channel"); **money** the founder-myth of quitting college to build a company ("the billionaire dropout"); **sport-games** a runner or player who quits a race or tournament ("three dropouts at mile twenty"); **law-state** a candidate who leaves a race ("he dropped out of the primary")
