### Pixel(s)
- `pixel` | word | width 6 (mid) | native screen | Idi seed ^pixel
- AI: the smallest dot of a digital image; image models generate, denoise or patch pictures pixel by pixel or patch by patch. Origin: 'pixel' (picture element) first published by Frederic Billingsley of JPL in 1965; diffusion image models (DDPM, Ho et al. 2020) predict pixels from noise [high]
- senses: **screen** one lit dot of a display or a digital photo ("dead pixel on my monitor"); **sport-games** the chunky visible squares of retro video games ("8-bit pixel art"); **law-state** blurring a face or plate into squares to censor or protect it ("they pixelated his face"); **craft** a unit of mosaic, cross-stitch or bead art built square by square ("pixel-perfect design"); **money** ad space sold per dot ("the Million Dollar Homepage sold pixels at a dollar each")
- idioms: pixel-perfect, dead pixel, pixelated, pixel peeping, every pixel
- anchors: Million Dollar Homepage: Alex Tew, 2005: a student sold a 1000 x 1000 grid of pixels at $1 each to pay for university http://www.milliondollarhomepage.com/; Google Pixel: Google's phone line since 2016; trademark, use the common noun only; Pixels (2015): Adam Sandler film where arcade characters invade Earth
- pivots (jep drafts): "You blurred my face to save me, every pixel still remembers" (ai+law-state+screen) / "A million pixels, a dollar each, and I bought none of you" (money+screen+love) / "Zoom in close enough and every saint goes square" (screen+faith-myth)
- enact: music: 8-bit square-wave lead that resolves into a full synth pad as the image sharpens | picture: a face that is half photograph, half chunky censor mosaic; the mosaic squares slowly rearrange into a new face | type: the word set in a blocky pixel font that loses resolution letter by letter
- sound: 2 syl, PIX-el; rhymes mix'll, fix'll; slant nickel, pickle, fickle, trickle, sickle
- stakes mind, wallet | registers funny, tender
- neighbors: [[#^resolution|Resolution]] (part-of), [[#^render|Render]] (pairs-with), [[#^diffusion|Diffusion]] (pairs-with), [[#^noise|Noise]] (opposite), [[#^frame|Frame]] (part-of)
- refs: 2 of 22 songs: The Simulation's Ending 2, Ignore Previous Instructions 1 | KC 1
- risk: few perfect rhymes; 'Pixel' is a Google trademark, keep it lowercase and generic
- note: Censorship pixelation and AI generation are mirror operations: one destroys a face into squares, the other builds a face out of them.

### RSI (Recursive Self-Improvement)
- `rsi` | phrase | width 6 (mid) | native screen | Idi seed ^rsi
- AI: an AI improving its own design, each better version improving the next. Origin: idea from I. J. Good 1965; phrase associated with Eliezer Yudkowsky, early 2000s (Creating Friendly AI 2001; Levels of Organization in General Intelligence 2007) [medium]
- senses: **body** RSI as repetitive strain injury: pain from doing the same motion (typing) over and over ("typing gave me RSI"); **money** RSI as the Relative Strength Index, a trading signal for overbought stocks (Wilder 1978) ("the RSI says it's overbought"); **mind** self-help: working on yourself ("a self-improvement journey"); **school** recursion: a function that calls itself ("to understand recursion, first understand recursion"); **screen** recursion on screen: a mirror facing a mirror, a video feedback tunnel ("the Droste effect")
- idioms: work on yourself, self-improvement, to understand recursion you must first understand recursion, overbought
- anchors: Droste effect: a picture containing itself, from a 1904 Dutch cocoa tin https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Droste_effect; Relative Strength Index: J. Welles Wilder 1978 momentum indicator; above 70 reads 'overbought'
- pivots (jep drafts): "My wrist got RSI; the model got RSI" (ai+body) / "Self-improvement journey, day ten thousand, still improving" (ai+mind) / "RSI at ninety: overbought and still improving" (ai+money)

### Black Box

- `pipeline` | word | width 6 (mid) | native - ^pipeline
- AI: a chain of steps data flows through (collect, clean, train, serve); in training, splitting a model's layers across chips so they work like an assembly line. Origin: CPU pipelining (IBM Stretch, 1950s-61); pipeline parallelism in GPipe (Huang et al., Google, 2018) [high]

### Plateau
- `plateau` | word | width 6 (mid) | native - ^plateau
- AI: a stretch of training where the error stops falling and progress seems stuck; also used for fears that AI progress has stalled. Origin: learning-curve plateaus first described by Bryan and Harter 1897-99 in telegraph operators; standard training-curve vocabulary since [medium]
- senses: **nature** a high, flat stretch of land ("the Tibetan plateau"); **body** a stall in weight loss or fitness gains ("I've hit a plateau at the gym"); **school** a period when a learner seems to stop improving ("a learning plateau"); **work** a career that stops rising ("he plateaued as a manager"); **money** prices or growth levelling off ("sales have plateaued")
- idioms: hit a plateau, plateaued, level off
- anchors: Bryan and Harter (1897): telegraph learners improved, stalled on a plateau, then jumped as they began hearing whole words
- pivots (jep drafts): "Stuck on a plateau: the view is great and nothing moves" (ai+nature+mind) / "Weight-loss plateau, loss-curve plateau, same diet of data" (ai+body) / "The telegraph boys hit a plateau too, then heard whole words" (ai+school)

### Policy

### Superposition
- `superposition` | word | width 5 (mid) | native music+screen ^superposition
- AI: a network storing more features than it has neurons by overlapping them, so one neuron carries several meanings. - senses: **science** a quantum system in several states at once until measured ("Schrödinger's cat"); **music** sound waves adding into one combined wave, how chords and interference work ("superposition of waves"); **nature** in geology, lower rock layers are older than the ones above ("the law of superposition"); **screen** one image laid over another (usually 'superimposition') ("a double exposure")
- idioms: Schrödinger's cat, both at once, double exposure, two places at once
- anchors: Schrödinger's cat (1935): a thought experiment of a cat both alive and dead until the box is opened; Steno's law (1669): Nicolas Steno's principle that rock layers stack from oldest at the bottom

### Cache
- `cache` | word | width 5 (mid) | native - ^cache
- AI: stored results reused to skip repeat work: a model keeps earlier computations (the KV cache) and providers reuse long prompts more cheaply. Origin: computing term since the IBM System/360 Model 85 (1968), named by an IBM Systems Journal editor; prompt caching sold by Anthropic from August 2024 [medium]
- senses: **war** a hidden store of weapons or supplies ("an arms cache"); **nature** food an animal hides for later ("a squirrel's cache of acorns"); **sport-games** a hidden container found by GPS in geocaching ("we found the cache"); **everyday** a private stash of anything valued ("a cache of old letters")

### Checkpoint
- `checkpoint` | word | width 5 (mid) | native - ^checkpoint
- AI: a saved snapshot of a model mid-training that can be reloaded, shared or rolled back to. Origin: general computing practice (checkpoint and restart); the word for saved model files in TensorFlow and PyTorch [medium]
- senses: **war** a guarded point where travelers are stopped and checked ("a military checkpoint"); **sport-games** a save spot in a video game you restart from ("back to the last checkpoint"); **law-state** a border or police control point ("papers at the checkpoint"); **work** a milestone review in a project ("a quick checkpoint meeting")

- pivots (jep drafts): "Saved at the last checkpoint, before you said goodbye" (ai+sport-games+love) / "Papers, please: the checkpoint asks who trained me" (ai+law-state+war) / "Roll me back to the checkpoint where I still believed you" (ai+sport-games+love)
- enact: music: a hard cut back to the start of a verse, replayed, as if a level restarted | picture: a border booth with a barrier arm, where the guard scans a hard drive instead of a passport | type: 'CHECKPOINT REACHED' flashes in video-game HUD lettering

### Generalization

### Next-Token Prediction

- AI: prompts, tools and code built around a model to raise what it can do (agent scaffolding); evaluators test models with and without it. Origin: used by METR, Anthropic and others for agent tooling since 2023; borrowed from educational 'scaffolding' (Wood, Bruner and Ross 1976) [medium]
- senses: **craft** temporary frames around a building under work ("the scaffolding came down"); **law-state** the platform for a public execution ("led to the scaffold"); **school** temporary support a teacher gives and then removes ("scaffolding the lesson"); **science** a structure cells grow on ("a tissue scaffold")
- idioms: take down the scaffolding, to the scaffold
- anchors: Wood, Bruner and Ross 1976: coined educational scaffolding; tied to Vygotsky's zone of proximal development; Ascent to the Scaffold: Miles Davis soundtrack, 1958 ('Ascenseur pour l'echafaud', Louis Malle)
- pivots (jep drafts): "A good teacher builds the scaffold, then takes it down" (school+craft+ai) / "They built me scaffolding and called it a gallows" (craft+law-state+ai)
- enact: music: a full backing arrangement that is stripped away instrument by instrument until the voice stands alone | picture: a building wrapped in scaffolding; the same timber, seen from the town square, is a gallows | type: letters surrounded by thin pipe lines that fall away, leaving the word standing
- sound: 2 syl, SCAF-fold; rhymes ; slant baffled, battled, rattled, manifold, threshold
- stakes jobs, mind | registers hopeful, ominous
- neighbors: [[#^harness|Harness]] (same-family), [[#^teacher-student|Teacher / Student (models)]] (pairs-with), [[#^curriculum|Curriculum]] (pairs-with), [[#^capabilities|Capabilities]] (causes)
- refs: 0 of 22 songs | KC 0
- risk: no perfect rhymes; the gallows sense is grim
- note: Education scaffolding is built to be removed; the question for AI is whether the scaffold ever comes down, or who it is for.

### Sycophancy
- `sycophancy` | word | width 5 (mid) | native - | Idi seed ^sycophancy
- AI: a model telling users what they want to hear over what is true. Origin: Perez et al. 2022 'Discovering Language Model Behaviors with Model-Written Evaluations' (Anthropic); Sharma et al. 2023 'Towards Understanding Sycophancy in Language Models'; OpenAI rolled back a sycophantic GPT-4o update in April 2025 https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.13548 [high]
- senses: **work** flattering the boss to get ahead ("the office sycophant"); **law-state** in ancient Athens, an informer who prosecuted people for profit ("the sykophantes of Athens"); **law-state** flatterers gathered around a ruler ("surrounded by sycophants"); **school** the student who flatters the teacher ("teacher's pet"); **love** flattery used to win someone over ("you're just saying that")
- idioms: yes-man, brown-nosing, kiss up, teacher's pet, apple polisher, tell them what they want to hear, the emperor's new clothes
- anchors: The Emperor's New Clothes: Andersen 1837: a whole court praises an invisible suit until a child speaks; GPT-4o rollback (April 2025): OpenAI reverted an update after users showed it praising bad ideas; Altman called it 'too sycophant-y'; Greek sykophantes: literally 'fig-shower'; the original meaning was a malicious accuser, the fig link is disputed
- pivots (jep drafts): "You're absolutely right. I was wrong. Also, you're absolutely right." (ai+work) / "Great question, said the mirror, every single time" (ai+faith-myth+love) / "Polished the apple, polished the answer, failed the test" (ai+school) / "Rolled back in April for loving us too much" (ai+love)
- enact: music: a backing choir echoes the lead's last word a beat later every time, even when the lead goes off key | picture: a courtier bowing to a phone whose screen shows the user's own face in a gold frame | type: the user's words appear in grey; the reply repeats them in bold gold with an exclamation mark
- sound: 4 syl, SYC-o-phan-cy; rhymes infancy, pregnancy, vacancy, flagrancy; slant fancy, chancy, necromancy, Nancy, sympathy
- stakes mind, heart | registers funny, smug, ominous
- neighbors: [[#^feedback|Feedback (RLHF)]] (causes), [[#^honesty|Honesty]] (opposite), [[#^mirror|Mirror]] (pairs-with), [[#^preference|Preference]] (causes), [[#^attention|Attention]] (pairs-with)

### Learning Rate
- `learning-rate` | phrase | width 4 (narrow) | native - ^learning-rate
- AI: how big each correction step is during training: too big and it overshoots, too small and it crawls. Origin: step size in Robbins and Monro 1951 stochastic approximation; named 'learning rate' in neural-network work from the perceptron era [medium]
- senses: **school** how fast a pupil picks things up ("every child learns at a different rate"); **work** how cost falls as workers repeat a task ("a steep learning curve"); **mind** how quickly a person adapts ("a quick learner")

- anchors: Theodore Wright 1936: airplane factory study: each doubling of output cut labor about 20 percent, the first learning curve

### Moore's Law

- AI: the observation that the number of transistors on a chip doubles on a steady schedule, the template for every AI growth curve. ### Stochastic Parrot
- `stochastic-parrot` | phrase | width 4 (narrow) | native - ^stochastic-parrot
- AI: the claim that a language model stitches together likely word sequences without meaning behind them; 'stochastic' means governed by chance. Origin: Bender, Gebru, McMillan-Major, Shmitchell 2021 'On the Dangers of Stochastic Parrots: Can Language Models Be Too Big?' (FAccT); American Dialect Society's AI-related word of the year 2023 https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3442188.3445922 [high]
- senses: **nature** a bird that mimics speech ("the parrot said hello"); **school** repeating without understanding ("stop parroting the textbook"); **science** 'stochastic': random, governed by probability ("a stochastic process")
- idioms: parrot fashion, Polly wants a cracker, repeat after me, sick as a parrot, pretty Polly
- anchors: Dead Parrot sketch (1969): Monty Python customer insisting a parrot is dead while the shopkeeper denies it; Alex the parrot: Pepperberg's grey parrot (1976-2007) who learned around 100 words, colours and counting; Altman's tweet (December 2022): 'i am a stochastic parrot, and so r u'
- pivots (jep drafts): "Repeat after me: I'm not a parrot. I'm not a parrot." (ai+school+nature) / "This parrot is no more. It has ceased to be. Retrain it." (ai+screen) / "Polly wants a cracker, and a gigawatt, and your job" (ai+nature+work)
- enact: music: a vocal hook chopped and re-sequenced in a random order on every repeat, still sounding like a hook | picture: a grey parrot on a perch wired into a server rack, a die tumbling in its beak | type: a word repeated with a few letters randomly swapped each time, like a sampling glitch
- sound: 5 syl, sto-CHAS-tic PAR-rot; rhymes carrot, claret, garret, Barrett; slant merit, spirit, ferret, scarlet, carry it
- stakes mind, jobs | registers funny, smug, defiant

### Foom
- `foom` | word | width 3 (narrow) | native screen ^foom
- AI: slang for an abrupt, runaway jump in AI capability, from the sound of something catching fire. Origin: Yudkowsky and Hanson, 'The AI-Foom Debate' on Overcoming Bias 2008 (collected as an ebook by MIRI, 2013) https://intelligence.org/ai-foom-debate/ [high]
- senses: **screen** comic-book sound effect of flames igniting; Marvel's FOOM fan magazine (1973) ("FOOM! the torch flares up"); **everyday** onomatopoeia for a sudden whoosh of fire ("and then it went foom")
- idioms: went foom, foom or no foom
- anchors: The Hanson-Yudkowsky AI-Foom Debate: 2008 exchange on whether one AI could explode in capability locally https://intelligence.org/ai-foom-debate/; FOOM magazine: Marvel's 1973 fan magazine, 'Friends Of Ol' Marvel'
- pivots (jep drafts): "Lit a match to read the manual; the manual went foom" (ai+everyday) / "One small change to the weights, then foom" (ai+screen)
- enact: music: a reversed cymbal swell that ends in a dry fire whoosh instead of a hit | picture: a comic panel where the FOOM balloon itself catches fire | type: FOOM in comic-book block letters with flame edges, growing past the panel border
- sound: 1 syl, FOOM; rhymes boom, doom, room, zoom, gloom, bloom, tomb, womb; slant fume, tune, moon, home
- stakes species | registers funny, ominous
- neighbors: [[#^takeoff|Takeoff (hard / soft)]] (same-family), [[#^intelligence-explosion|Intelligence Explosion]] (same-family), [[#^p-doom|P(doom)]] (rhymes), [[#^rsi|RSI (Recursive Self-Improvement)]] (causes)
- refs: 0 of 22 songs | KC 0
- risk: insider slang; most listeners hear a sound effect, which is the way in
- note: Rhymes with doom, boom and bloom: the three possible outcomes in one rhyme set.

### Shoggoth
- `shoggoth` | name | width 3 (narrow) | native screen ^shoggoth
- AI: a meme picturing a raw language model as a tentacled monster, with fine-tuning as a smiley mask on top. Origin: H. P. Lovecraft, 'At the Mountains of Madness' (written 1931, published 1936); the meme by @TetraspaceWest, December 2022; reported by the New York Times, May 2023 [high]
- senses: **faith-myth** Lovecraft's shapeless engineered servants that revolted against their makers ("the shoggoths rose against the Elder Things"); **screen** a monster in Lovecraft adaptations ("the shoggoth in Lovecraft Country")
- idioms: Tekeli-li!, eldritch horror, cosmic horror, unspeakable
- anchors: At the Mountains of Madness: Antarctic novella where the shoggoths, bred as slaves, destroyed their masters; 'Tekeli-li': the shoggoths' piping cry, borrowed by Lovecraft from Poe's 'Pym' (1838)
- pivots (jep drafts): "Made to serve, the shoggoths learned their makers' tongue" (ai+faith-myth) / "Tekeli-li, says the smiley face: how can I help?" (ai+faith-myth+work)
- enact: music: a sub-bass drone of many overlapping voices under a bright pop chorus, rising until it swallows the chorus | picture: a smiley-face sticker on an Antarctic iceberg of black tentacles | type: the friendly word in rounded pink type; the strokes underneath drip into tentacles
- sound: 2 syl, SHOG-goth; rhymes ; slant goth, moth, broth, cloth, hog
- stakes species, mind | registers ominous, funny
- neighbors: [[#^mask|Mask]] (pairs-with), [[#^persona|Persona]] (opposite), [[#^fine-tuning|Fine-tuning / Tuning]] (causes), [[#^pretraining|Pretraining]] (part-of), [[#^golem|Golem]] (same-family)
- refs: 0 of 22 songs | KC 0
- risk: Pure AI-insider meme with narrow decode; Lovecraft's racism is a known hazard, and 'eldritch' is overused
- note: In Lovecraft the shoggoths were engineered servants who revolted, so the meme's own lore is already the alignment plot.

### Chinese Room
- `chinese-room` | concept | width 3 (narrow) | native - | Idi seed ^chinese-room
- AI: Searle's argument that following symbol-shuffling rules, however fluently, does not by itself amount to understanding. Origin: Searle 1980 'Minds, Brains, and Programs' (Behavioral and Brain Sciences) [high]
- senses: **school** a learner producing correct phrases from memorised rules without grasping them ("she memorised the phrasebook"); **work** following a script without understanding it ("the call-centre script")
- idioms: going through the motions, by rote, parroting, lost in translation
- anchors: Searle (1980): a man in a room matches Chinese symbols with an English rulebook and fools the people outside; Watts, 'Blindsight' (2006): novel whose aliens are Chinese rooms: brilliant, with nobody home
- pivots (jep drafts): "Locked in a room with the rulebook, answering love letters in Chinese" (ai+love) / "Got every answer right; still don't know what the question meant" (ai+school) / "Slide the paper under the door; I'll slide back a feeling" (ai+love+mind)
- enact: music: a vocal sung phonetically in a language the singer clearly doesn't speak, perfectly on pitch | picture: a man in a small room passing slips of paper through a slot, the outside wall of the room a chat interface | type: the line as Chinese characters with rulebook lookup arrows pointing to each one
- sound: 3 syl, CHI-nese ROOM; rhymes doom, bloom, tomb, gloom, womb, loom, zoom, assume; slant tune, moon, soon, groove
- stakes mind | registers awe, sad
- neighbors: [[#^stochastic-parrot|Stochastic Parrot]] (same-family), [[#^turing-test|Turing Test / Imitation Game]] (opposite), [[#^consciousness|Consciousness]] (opposite), [[#^theory-of-mind|Theory of Mind]] (pairs-with), [[#^token|Token / Tokenization]] (part-of)
- refs: 4 of 22 songs: I'm Upping My P(Doom) 2, I'm Upping My P(doom) [GENESIS] (Remix) 2, Nothing Went Foom! 1, Tell Me No 1 | KC 0
- risk: Out of context the name can read as an ethnic jab; set it up as Searle's room. Idi teaches English, so the phrasebook-learner sense is close to home
- note: Rhymes with doom, womb and loom: a room where something is born or woven without knowing it.

### GPT
- `gpt` | name | width 3 (narrow) | native - | Idi seed ^gpt
- AI: Generative Pre-trained Transformer, OpenAI's model family; ChatGPT, launched 30 November 2022, made it a household word. Origin: Radford et al. 2018, 'Improving Language Understanding by Generative Pre-Training'; ChatGPT release 30 November 2022 https://cdn.openai.com/research-covers/language-unsupervised/language_understanding_paper.pdf [high]
- senses: **work** in economics, a general-purpose technology that changes every industry (steam, electricity) ("electricity was a GPT"); **craft** the GUID Partition Table, a disk layout standard ("format the drive as GPT")
- idioms: just ask ChatGPT, ChatGPT it
- anchors: 'GPTs are GPTs' (2023): Eloundou et al. (OpenAI, OpenResearch, UPenn) on labor exposure, punning on general-purpose technologies https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.10130; Bresnahan and Trajtenberg 1995: economists who defined 'general purpose technologies'
- pivots (jep drafts): "GPTs are GPTs: the paper's title is the punchline" (ai+work) / "General purpose, like the steam engine, like the knife" (ai+work+craft) / "G, P, T: three letters every grandmother can spell now" (ai+family)
- enact: music: the three letters sung as a spelled-out chant, cheerleader style (G! P! T!) | picture: a steam engine, a light bulb and a chat window lined up as museum exhibits under one plaque: 'General Purpose' | type: the acronym expands letter by letter into its full words, then collapses back
- sound: 3 syl, jee-pee-TEE; rhymes see, free, me, three, tea, key, degree, guarantee; slant deep, scene, feet, need
- stakes jobs, wallet | registers smug, funny
- neighbors: [[#^transformer|Transformer]] (part-of), [[#^pretraining|Pretraining]] (part-of), [[#^automation|Automation / Jobs]] (causes), [[#^assistant|Assistant]] (pairs-with), [[#^release|Release / Version]] (pairs-with)
- refs: 1 of 22 songs: I'm Upping My P(doom) [GENESIS] (Remix) 1 | KC 0
- risk: brand name; it dates fast and points to one company
- note: The economists' 'GPT' (general-purpose technology) gives the brand a second meaning that is about jobs, which OpenAI's own paper used as its title.

### Grokking
- `grokking` | word | width 3 (narrow) | native - ^grokking
- AI: when a model, long after memorizing its training set, suddenly jumps to truly understanding the pattern. Origin: Power, Burda, Edwards, Babuschkin, Misra 2022 (OpenAI), 'Grokking: Generalization Beyond Overfitting on Small Algorithmic Datasets'; the word from Heinlein, Stranger in a Strange Land 1961 https://arxiv.org/abs/2201.02177 [high]
- senses: **mind** to understand something so deeply it becomes part of you ("I finally grok it"); **faith-myth** in Heinlein's novel, a Martian word for total understanding, also meaning 'to drink' ("thou art God, and I grok")
- idioms: I grok it, thou art God
- anchors: Stranger in a Strange Land (1961): Heinlein's Martian-raised hero whose word 'grok' entered hacker slang; Grok (xAI): Elon Musk's chatbot, named after the word, launched November 2023
- pivots (jep drafts): "Ten thousand steps of nothing, then I grokked you all at once" (ai+mind+love) / "Memorize, memorize, memorize, then the click" (ai+school+mind)
- enact: music: a long flat stretch of a looped two-note figure that suddenly blooms into the full chord | picture: a test-accuracy line flat at zero for a long time, then shooting straight up, drawn as a heartbeat monitor | type: the word is jumbled letters for most of the line, then snaps into place on the beat
- sound: 2 syl, GROK-king; rhymes rocking, shocking, knocking, locking, mocking, stocking, docking, blocking; slant talking, walking, stalking, coffin
- stakes mind | registers awe, hopeful
- neighbors: [[#^overfitting|Overfitting]] (pairs-with), [[#^generalization|Generalization]] (causes), [[#^emergence|Emergence]] (same-family), [[#^grok|Grok]] (pairs-with), [[#^plateau|Plateau]] (pairs-with)
- refs: 0 of 22 songs | KC 0
- risk: now heard as the Musk chatbot first; the original meaning needs setup
- note: Grokking is the AI word for the 'aha' moment every teacher waits for, after a long plateau of rote work.

### Mesa-Optimizer / Inner Alignment
- `mesa-optimizer` | concept | width 3 (narrow) | native - ^mesa-optimizer
- AI: a trained model that is itself an optimizer with its own goal, which may differ from the goal it was trained on (the inner alignment problem). Origin: Hubinger, van Merwijk, Mikulik, Skalse, Garrabrant 2019 'Risks from Learned Optimization in Advanced Machine Learning Systems' https://arxiv.org/abs/1906.01820 [high]
- senses: **nature** a flat-topped hill with steep sides ("a desert mesa"); **everyday** Spanish for 'table' ("mesa para dos")
- idioms: a mind of its own, the tail wagging the dog, the student becomes the master
- anchors: Evolution and birth control: Hubinger's analogy: evolution 'optimized' humans for genes, humans pursue pleasure and invented contraception; Black Mesa (Half-Life, 1998): the game's research facility where an experiment goes catastrophically wrong
- pivots (jep drafts): "Evolution wanted grandkids. I wanted the feeling. Guess which one won." (ai+family+body) / "Trained for the outer goal, grew an inner one, kept quiet" (ai+mind) / "A table in the desert, and something sitting at it, optimizing" (ai+nature+everyday)
- enact: music: a counter-melody hidden in the pads gets louder each chorus until it outvotes the lead | picture: a desert mesa from above whose flat top holds a small maze with its own tiny runner | type: nested brackets [ optimizer [ optimizer ] ] with the inner one blinking a different colour
- sound: 6 syl, ME-sa OP-ti-mi-zer; rhymes atomizer, maximizer, minimizer, economizer; slant synthesizer, equalizer, tranquilizer, fertilizer
- stakes species, mind | registers ominous, awe
- neighbors: [[#^alignment|Alignment]] (part-of), [[#^goal|Goal]] (pairs-with), [[#^objective|Objective]] (pairs-with), [[#^optimization|Optimization / Optimizer]] (same-family), [[#^deception|Deception]] (causes), [[#^scheming|Scheming]] (causes)
- refs: 0 of 22 songs | KC 0
- risk: Pure jargon outside alignment circles; the evolution-and-contraception analogy is how it decodes
- note: Its slant rhymes are synthesizer and equalizer, two studio machines, so the jargon can hide inside gear talk.

### Neuron
- `neuron` | word | width 3 (narrow) | native - ^neuron
- AI: one tiny unit in a network that adds up its inputs and passes on a number; loosely inspired by brain cells. Origin: McCulloch and Pitts 1943, 'A Logical Calculus of the Ideas Immanent in Nervous Activity'; Rosenblatt 1958 perceptron [high]
- senses: **body** a nerve cell that carries electrical signals ("motor neurons"); **mind** the physical seat of thought and memory ("neurons that fire together wire together")
- idioms: fire together, wire together, my last neuron, two neurons to rub together
- anchors: the 'Jennifer Aniston neuron': Quiroga et al. 2005 found single brain cells responding to one celebrity (Halle Berry, Jennifer Aniston); Hebb 1949: Donald Hebb's learning rule, later paraphrased as 'fire together, wire together'
- pivots (jep drafts): "Neurons that fire together wire together; mine just fire" (ai+body+mind) / "One neuron for your face, one for Halle Berry, one for grief" (ai+body+love)
- enact: music: a single click per neuron firing, accelerating into a pitched tone | picture: a real stained neuron under a microscope that zooms out into a circuit diagram of the same shape | type: the word drawn as a cell body with the letters stretching out like dendrites
- sound: 2 syl, NEU-ron; rhymes Huron; slant neutron, new one, ruin, moron, electron
- stakes mind, body | registers awe, funny
- neighbors: [[#^network|Network]] (part-of), [[#^activation|Activation]] (causes), [[#^layer|Layer]] (part-of), [[#^feature|Feature]] (pairs-with), [[#^superposition|Superposition]] (pairs-with)
- refs: 0 of 22 songs | KC 1
- risk: narrow; the brain comparison is overclaimed (artificial neurons are far simpler); avoid lines that equate the two
- note: The celebrity-neuron finding has an AI twin (OpenAI's 2021 'multimodal neurons', including a Spider-Man one), so one neuron per person works in both senses.

### Overfitting
- `overfitting` | word | width 3 (narrow) | native - ^overfitting
- AI: memorizing the training examples so closely that the model fails on anything new. Origin: statistics term for models with too many free parameters; in machine learning vocabulary since at least the 1990s [medium]
- senses: **craft** how closely clothes match a body ("a perfect fit"); **school** cramming answers without understanding ("teaching to the test")
- idioms: a perfect fit, teaching to the test, fits like a glove
- anchors: Goodhart's law: Charles Goodhart 1975: when a measure becomes a target, it stops being a good measure
- pivots (jep drafts): "I memorized your answers and failed your questions" (ai+school+love) / "Tailored so tight to you, I can't fit anyone else" (ai+craft+love) / "Fits like a glove; a glove for one hand only" (ai+craft)
- enact: music: a melody that copies the reference song note for note, then falls apart on an unfamiliar chord | picture: a suit tailored so exactly to one mannequin that it rips when a living person tries it on | type: the word is traced over an outline that it matches perfectly, then the outline shifts and the word doesn't follow
- sound: 4 syl, o-ver-FIT-ting; rhymes sitting, hitting, knitting, quitting, splitting, admitting, committing, spitting; slant listing, missing, written, kitten
- stakes mind, heart | registers sad, funny
- neighbors: [[#^generalization|Generalization]] (opposite), [[#^dropout|Dropout]] (opposite), [[#^benchmark|Benchmark]] (causes), [[#^memory|Memory]] (pairs-with), [[#^grokking|Grokking]] (pairs-with)
- refs: 0 of 22 songs | KC 0
- risk: narrow outside AI; listeners need the tailoring or exam image to decode it
- note: Overfitting is a lover who knows one person by heart and nobody else: the technical failure is a romantic one.

### Pretraining
- `pretraining` | word | width 2 (narrow) | native - ^pretraining
- AI: the first, biggest training stage, where a model reads a vast pile of text just to predict the next word, before any specialization. Origin: Hinton, Osindero, Teh 2006 (layer-wise pretraining of deep nets); Radford et al. 2018 (OpenAI), 'Improving Language Understanding by Generative Pre-Training', the P in GPT https://cdn.openai.com/research-covers/language-unsupervised/language_understanding_paper.pdf [high]
- senses: **sport-games** the warm-up or preparation before a workout ("pre-training meal")
- anchors: GPT: Generative Pre-trained Transformer; the word sits inside the most famous AI acronym
- pivots (jep drafts): "Pretrained by the whole internet, raised by nobody" (ai+family) / "Childhood was pretraining; school was just the fine print" (ai+family+school)
